, just like the operator
-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
acting on functions without any restriction to spherical harmonics of higher degree. From this, it follows that the estimates described in
Step 4
apply, where
\phi_{\geq\ell}
with the above modified angular operator takes on the role of
\phi_{\geq 0}
with the standard operator
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
and
\alpha+\ell(\ell+1)
replaces
\alpha
, which is equivalent to
\beta_{\ell}
replacing
\beta_{0}
everywhere.

Step 5
is carried out in more detail in §
11
.

4.2.1
Comparison with previous physical space methods

While not included in the present paper, the strategy described in Steps 1–5 above applies also to the setting of the equation

\square_{g_{M}}\phi=V\phi,

where
|V|\leq r^{-2-\delta}
,
\delta>0
(including
V\equiv 0
). Additionally,
Steps

2
–
5
apply also when
g_{M}
is replaced by suitable, more general asymptotically flat metrics
g
and an inverse-square (or more rapidly decaying potential), assuming that analogues of energy boundedness and integrated energy estimates (see
Steps 0
and
1
) hold. We outline here briefly the key ideas in the case
V\equiv 0
and
g=g_{M}
.

One can obtain the leading-order late-time behaviour of
\phi
for sufficiently rapidly decaying data by considering

T^{-1}\widehat{\phi}=T^{-1}\phi-\Phi_{0}[\phi](\tau+1+2r)^{-1}(\tau+1)^{-1},

where

\Phi_{0}[\phi]=4I_{0}^{(1)}[\phi]

with
I_{0}^{(1)}[\phi]
the
time-inverted Newman–Penrose constant
of
\phi
or equivalently, the (standard) Newman–Penrose constant of
T^{-1}\phi
; see §
1.1
.
13
13

13

In particular,
I_{0}^{(1)}[\phi]
is well-defined for initial data with vanishing Newman–Penrose constant. If the Newman–Penrose constant
I_{0}[\phi]
is instead non-vanishing, we instead consider
\widehat{\phi}=\phi-\Phi_{0}(\tau+1+2r)^{-1}(\tau+1)^{-1}
and take
\Phi_{0}=4I_{0}[\phi]
.

One can show that
T^{-1}\widehat{\phi}
decays with a better rate than the (expected) sharp decay rate of
T^{-1}\phi
. For this, we construct
T^{-2}{\phi}
and we show that the
r
-weighted energy estimates from
Step 2
with
N=2
apply when we replace
\phi
with
T^{-2}\phi
and the bottleneck for the decay rate arises from the initial data asymptotics of
T^{-2}\phi
, which feature
I_{0}^{(1)}[\phi]
in the leading-order asymptotic term as
r\to\infty
. Hence, by taking
\Phi_{0}[\phi]
to be an appropriate multiple of
I_{0}^{(1)}[\phi]
,
T^{-2}\widehat{\phi}
will decay faster in
r
at
\tau=0
than
T^{-2}\phi
and, consequently, we can show that
\widehat{\phi}
decays with a faster rate than the sharp decay rate for
\phi
.

The above procedure stands in contrast with the method introduced in
[
AAG18a
]
where late-time asymptotics are instead obtained by propagating the conservation law for the quantity

r^{2}X(rT^{-1}\phi_{0})|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(\tau)

in
\tau
to a suitable region near infinity and then extending the late-time asymptotics towards the horizon. The latter method requires in particular commuting the
r
-weighted estimates of
Step 2
with vector fields that behave to leading-order like
r^{2}L
, whereas the method presented in the present paper involves instead an additional application of
T^{-1}
. The application of
T^{-1}
and
r^{2}L
each result in a decrease of the sharp decay rate of the
L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})
-norm of
r\phi
by exactly one power. However, as the method developed in the present paper does not require the existence of conservation laws along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, it is more widely applicable.

4.3
Other related works

In this section, we mention further works that are related to the present paper, but did not feature in the discussion in §
1.1
.

Wave equations on asymptotically flat black hole spacetimes

The physical-space method for proving decay estimates in the present paper involves important techniques that were introduced in the literature in the setting of wave equations on asymptotically flat black hole spacetimes, most notably, the red-shift estimates of
[
DR09
]
, the method of
r
-weighted estimates introduced in
[
DR10
]
, the integrated energy estimates or Morawetz estimates in
[
BS09
,
DR09
]
and the hierarchy of elliptic estimates from
[
AAG21a
,
AAG21b
]
. We note that many of these ideas are sufficiently robust so that they can been applied in much more general settings: in the study of wave equations on Kerr spacetimes, see
[
DRSR16
,
AAG21a
]
and references therein, as well as in the study of the Teukolsky equations and linearized gravity; see for example the recent developments
[
DHR19b
,
DHR19a
,
Ma20
,
SRTdC20
,
MZ21b
]
, and even in the setting of the nonlinear Einstein equations
[
KS20
,
DHRT21
]
.

Fourier-analytic methods

The method outlined in §
4.2
is based entirely in physical space. One can alternatively approach the question of late-time asymptotics and the existence of inverse-polynomial late-time tails in Fourier space.

The corresponding inverse-polynomial decay rates are related to the regularity of the resolvent operator at the zero time-frequency. The resolvent operator can be viewed as the inverse of the operator
\square_{g_{S}}-V_{\alpha}
with time derivatives
T
(with respect to an asymptotically hyperboloidal time slicing) replaced by multiplication with
-i\omega
with
\omega\in\mathbb{C}
denoting the frequency with respect to the choice of time function.

This fact was exploited in a heuristic setting in
[
Lea86
]
to arrive at late-time tails for the wave equation without a potential on Schwarzschild. In
[
Hin22
]
, a mathematically rigorous argument was provided to infer the late-time leading-order behaviour on Schwarzschild and Kerr spacetimes, as well as on the Minkowski spacetime with a potential satisfying
|V|\lesssim r^{-3}
, from a careful analysis of the resolvent operator in a neighbourhood of
\omega=0
. We note that for the special case
\omega=0
, the resolvent operator coincides with the inverse of
\mathcal{L}
appearing in the construction of time-integral data in Step 3. See also earlier work
[
Tat13
]
where sharp decay estimates are obtained via the same philosophy in a general setting of stationary spacetimes, assuming a priori appropriate integrated energy estimates. We point the reader to
[
Hin22
]
for a detailed overview of further references to results pertaining to low-energy resolvent estimates.

A similar point of view was taken in
[
DSS12
,
DSS11
]
where the behaviour near the zero time-frequency of a resolvent operator is studied using techniques adapted to the framework of the wave equation in one space dimension with appropriate potential, in order to derive refined decay-in-time estimates for fixed spherical harmonic modes
\phi_{\ell}
on Schwarzschild with
V_{\alpha}\equiv 0
. Within a similar one-dimensional setting, let us also mention
[
DS10
,
CH15
]
, where sharp decay estimates are obtained for wave equations on the real line with potential
V
satisfying
|V|\lesssim|x|^{-p}
, where
p>2
, and
[
CSST08
,
DK16
]
, where the
p=2
case is considered. See also
[
Sch21
]
, where the low-energy behaviour of Schrödinger operators derived from
[
CSST08
]
is used to obtain sharp decay-in-time estimates for the one-dimensional wave equation with a model inverse-square potential satisfying assumption
A’)
in §
3.1
, featuring decay rates that are analogous to the rates appearing in the precise leading-order asymptotics in Theorem
4.1
. We refer to
[
Sch07
,
Sch21
]
for a further overview of literature pertaining to time-decay estimates for one-dimensional wave equations with inverse-polynomially decaying potentials.

Exactly inverse-square potentials on Minkowski

In
[
PST03
,
BPST03
,
BPST04
]
, the time-decay properties for wave equations with the singular potentials
V_{\alpha}(r)=\alpha r^{-2}
, where
\alpha>-\frac{1}{4}
, on
3+1
-dimensional Minkowski spacetime backgrounds (i.e. with respect to the standard wave operator), as well as natural higher-dimensional analogues, were studied extensively. In these works, weighted
L^{2}
estimates and Strichartz estimates were derived and applied to the analysis of several nonlinear problems. A different approach for obtaining decay-in-time estimates is provided in
[
GVdM22
]
, exploiting instead a connection of the conformal structure of Minkowski with
AdS_{2}
geometries to derive the leading-order late-time asymptotics from local existence theory for wave equations on
AdS_{2}
spacetimes and obtaining the analogue of Theorem
4.1
for the wave equation with singular potential
V_{\alpha}(r)=\alpha r^{-2}
on the
3+1
-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.

5
Uniform energy boundedness

We start by deriving uniform boundedness for energy quantities that degenerate at the event horizon at
r=2M
. We define the
T
-energy
of
\phi
as follows:

E_{T}[\phi](\tau):=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}Dr^{2}(X\phi)^{2}+h\tilde{h}r^{2}(T\phi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}+\phi^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

Observe that
E_{T}[\phi]
degenerates at
r=2M
due to the presence of
D(r)=1-\frac{2M}{r}
.

We moreover define the
renormalized
T
-energy
for
\phi_{\ell}
as follows:

\check{E}_{T}[\phi_{\ell}](\tau):=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}Dw_{\ell}^{2}r^{2}(X\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+h\tilde{h}r^{2}w^{2}_{\ell}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr,

with the weight functions
w_{\ell}
and the renormalizations
\check{\phi}_{\ell}
defined in §
3.3
. We will moreover denote
E_{T}[\phi]:=~E_{T}[\phi](0)
and
\check{E}_{T}[\phi_{\ell}]:=\check{E}_{T}[\phi_{\ell}](0)
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})\cap C^{1}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}})
be a solution to (
3.1
). Then there exists a constant
C=C(V_{\alpha})>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\displaystyle E_{T}[\phi](\tau_{2})+\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cup\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}(T\phi)^{2}r^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\leq

\displaystyle\>CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1}),

(5.1)

\displaystyle\check{E}_{T}[\phi_{\ell}](\tau_{2})+\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cup\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}w_{\ell}^{2}r^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau=

\displaystyle\>\check{E}_{T}[\phi_{\ell}](\tau_{1}).

(5.2)

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C=C(V_{\alpha})>0
such that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\check{E}_{T}[\phi_{\ell}](\tau)\leq CE_{T}[\phi_{\ell}](\tau).

(5.3)

Proof.

We first multiply both sides of (
3.2
) with
-T\phi
and integrate by parts to obtain:

0=\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}X\left(-Dr^{2}X\phi T\phi+r^{2}(1-h)(T\phi)^{2}\right)+T\left(\frac{1}{2}h\tilde{h}r^{2}(T\phi)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}Dr^{2}(X\phi)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}r^{2}V_{\alpha}\phi^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau.

If we do not have the non-negativity property
V_{\alpha}\geq 0
, we apply (
2.10
) with
q=0
, together with
\psi\in C^{1}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}})
to estimate

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{1}{2}r^{2}V_{\alpha}\phi^{2}\,d\sigma dr\geq 4\min\{\inf_{r^{\prime}\in[2M,\infty)}r^{\prime 2}V_{\alpha}(r^{\prime}),0\}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{1}{2}D^{2}r^{2}(X\phi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr,

so coercivity of
E_{T}[\phi](\tau)
follows from assumption
A)
, which allow us to absorb the right-hand side above, and we obtain (
5.1
).

In order to obtain (
5.2
), we instead multiply both sides of (
3.10
) with
-T\check{\phi}_{\ell}
and integrate by parts to obtain:

0=\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}X\left(-Dw_{\ell}^{2}r^{2}X\check{\phi}_{\ell}T\check{\phi}_{\ell}+r^{2}(1-h)w_{\ell}^{2}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\right)+T\left(Dw_{\ell}^{2}r^{2}(X\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}h\tilde{h}r^{2}w_{\ell}^{2}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Finally, (
5.3
) follows straightforwardly from the definition of
\check{\phi}_{\ell}
.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

We only invoked the global assumption
r^{2}V_{\alpha}>-\frac{1}{4}
(assumption
A)
) to obtain (
5.1
). Energy boundedness of the renomalized energy (
5.2
) does
not
require this assumption.

6
Integrated energy estimates

In this section, we derive the integrated energy estimates that are necessary for establishing pointwise energy decay estimates in time. Though the main results of this paper invoke the assumption that
\alpha\neq 0
, the estimates in this section hold also for
\alpha=0
and they provide an alternative way of obtaining integrated energy estimates for (
1.1
), compared to existing methods in the literature. We start by deriving
local
integrated energy estimates for the renormalized quantities
\check{\phi}_{\ell}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})\cap C^{1}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}})
be a solution to (
3.1
). Let
R>2M
be arbitrarily large. Then there exists a constant
C=C(\ell,V_{\alpha},h,R)>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq\infty
:

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(DX\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(X\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau

(6.1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\check{E}_{T}[\phi_{\ell}](\tau_{1}),

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1-\delta}(DX\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau

(6.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\check{E}_{T}[\phi_{\ell}](\tau_{1})

Proof.

Let
f:[2M,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
be a differentiable function, to be determined later. We multiply both sides of (
3.9
) with
f(r)Z\check{\phi}_{\ell}
and apply the Leibniz rule to obtain:

\begin{split}0=&\>\frac{1}{2}fZ((Z\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2})+2f(r^{-1}+w_{\ell}^{\prime}w_{\ell}^{-1})(Z\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}-fT^{2}\check{\phi}_{\ell}Z\check{\phi}_{\ell}\\
=&\>Z\left(\frac{f}{2}(Z\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{f}{2}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\right)-T\left(fT\check{\phi}_{\ell}Z\check{\phi}_{\ell}\right)+\left[2fDr^{-1}+2fDw_{\ell}^{\prime}w_{\ell}^{-1}-\frac{1}{2}D\frac{df}{dr}\right](Z\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}D\frac{df}{dr}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}.\end{split}

The above equation is equivalent to:

\begin{split}0=&\>X\left(\frac{f}{2}(Z\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{f}{2}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\right)-T\left(\frac{f}{2}(1-h)(Z\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{f}{2}(1-h)(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+fT\check{\phi}_{\ell}Z\check{\phi}_{\ell}\right)\\
&+\left[2fr^{-1}+2fw_{\ell}^{\prime}w_{\ell}^{-1}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{df}{dr}\right](Z\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{df}{dr}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}.\end{split}

By (
3.8
), it follows that

2r^{-1}+2w_{\ell}^{\prime}w_{\ell}^{-1}=(1+\beta_{\ell})r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1-\beta_{\ell}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-2}),

so by taking
f(r)=r^{-q}
with
q>0
suitably large, depending on
w_{\ell}
, we obtain the following inequality for all
r\in[2M,\infty)
:

2fr^{-1}+2fw_{\ell}^{\prime}w_{\ell}^{-1}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{df}{dr}>0.

After integrating over
\bigcup_{\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]}\Sigma_{\tau}
, the contribution of the total
T
-derivative results moreover in boundary terms at
\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}
,
i=1,2
, which can be estimated via Proposition
5.1
after applying the identity:

(1-h)(Z\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+(1-h)(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+2T\check{\phi}_{\ell}Z\check{\phi}_{\ell}=(1-h)^{2}D^{2}(X\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}-(2-h)(1-h)h(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+2D(2-h)hX\check{\phi}_{\ell}T\check{\phi}_{\ell},

which follows from (
2.3
). We conclude that (
6.1
) holds.

In order to obtain (
6.2
), we subsequently take
q=\delta
, with
\delta>0
arbitrarily small and combine the resulting estimate with the already established local integrated energy estimate (
6.1
).
∎

In order to pass from the integrated energy estimates for
\check{\phi}_{\ell}
in Proposition
6.1
to an integrated energy estimate for
\phi
, we need to consider separately the cases:

A)

\alpha\leq 0

and

\ell=0
and

B)

\alpha>0

or

\ell\geq 1
.

6.0.1
\alpha\leq 0
and
\ell=0

In this section, we consider the case
\alpha\leq 0
and restrict to zeroth angular frequency modes
\phi_{0}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
1-\beta_{0}<p<1+\beta_{0}
. Then, for
R>2M
suitably large depending on
M
and
V_{\alpha}
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,R,V_{\alpha})>0
such that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}&r^{p}(L\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p-1}(L\psi_{0})^{2}+r^{p-3}\psi_{0}^{2}+r^{p-3}h_{0}(T\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{p-2}\psi_{0}^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p}(L\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{T}[\phi_{0}](\tau_{1}).\end{split}

(6.3)

Proof.

We will multiply both sides of (
3.4
) with
-r^{p}L\psi
:

\begin{split}0=&\>2r^{p}\underline{L}((L\psi)^{2})-Dr^{p-2}L\psi\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi+\frac{1}{2}D(V_{\alpha}r^{p}+D^{\prime}r^{p-1})L(\psi^{2})\\
=&\>\underline{L}\left[2r^{p}(L\psi)^{2}\right]+L\left[\frac{1}{2}D(V_{\alpha}r^{p}+D^{\prime}r^{p-1})\psi^{2}+\frac{1}{2}Dr^{p-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]\\
&+pDr^{p-1}(L\psi)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}D\frac{d}{dr}\left(DV_{\alpha}r^{p}\right)\psi^{2}-\frac{1}{4}D\frac{d}{dr}\left(Dr^{p-2}\right)|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\\
=&\>DX\left[-r^{p}(L\psi)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}D(V_{\alpha}r^{p}+D^{\prime}r^{p-1})\psi^{2}+\frac{1}{4}Dr^{p-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]\\
&+DT\left[\tilde{h}r^{p}(L\psi)^{2}+\frac{h}{4}D(V_{\alpha}r^{p}+D^{\prime}r^{p-1})\psi^{2}+\frac{h}{4}Dr^{p-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]\\
&+pDr^{p-1}(L\psi)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}D\frac{d}{dr}\left(DV_{\alpha}r^{p}\right)\psi^{2}-\frac{1}{4}D\frac{d}{dr}\left(Dr^{p-2}\right)|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}.\end{split}

(6.4)

We integrate the above equality over
\bigcup_{\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]}\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}
, using that
\psi=\psi_{0}
, and we apply (
5.1
) in combination with (
6.1
) and (
5.3
) to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}&\tilde{h}r^{p}(L\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{p}(L\psi_{0})^{2}-\frac{1}{4}(\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))r^{p-2}\psi_{0}^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}pr^{p-1}(L\psi_{0})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(2-p)r^{p-3}[\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\psi_{0}^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\tilde{h}r^{p}(L\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{T}[\phi_{0}](\tau_{1}).\end{split}

Note that by (
2.10
), we can estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\frac{1}{4}(2-p)r^{p-3}(|\alpha|+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))\psi_{0}^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq\frac{|\alpha|}{2-p}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p-3}(1+Cr^{-1})(X\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(|\alpha|r^{p-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3}))\psi_{0}^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\Big|_{r=R},\end{split}

(6.5)

for some constant
C=C(M,p,V_{\alpha})>0
.

We can further apply Young’s inequality to estimate for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small:

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\frac{1}{4}pr^{p-1}(DX\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(1+\epsilon)pr^{p-1}(L\psi_{0})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(1+\epsilon^{-1})h^{2}r^{p-1}(T\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

By (
6.2
) and (
5.3
), we can estimate

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}h^{2}r^{p-1}(T\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq CE_{T}[\phi_{0}](\tau_{1}),

provided we restrict to
p-5<-2-\beta_{0}
, or equivalently
p<3-\beta_{0}
.

We can therefore absorb the spacetime integral on the right-hand side of (
6.5
) into the left-hand side if we take

\frac{|\alpha|}{2-p}<\frac{1}{4}p,

which is equivalent to
1-\beta_{0}<p<1+\beta_{0}
. Note that
3-\beta_{0}\leq 1+\beta_{0}
for
\alpha\leq 0
.

It remains to group and estimate the various boundary terms on
\{r=R\}
. It is here that we will make use of Proposition
6.1
.

We apply Young’s inequality with
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small together with (
3.8
) to obtain:

\begin{split}(L\psi)^{2}=&\>2(L(rw_{0}\check{\phi}_{0}))^{2}\geq 2(1-\epsilon)\frac{D^{2}}{4}\left(\frac{d(rw_{0})}{dr}\right)^{2}\check{\phi}_{0}^{2}-2(\epsilon^{-1}-1)r^{2}w_{0}^{2}(L\check{\phi}_{0})^{2}\\
\geq&\>\left[\frac{1}{8}(1+\beta_{0})^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right]r^{-2}\psi^{2}-2(\epsilon^{-1}-1)r^{2}w_{0}^{2}(L\check{\phi}_{0})^{2},\end{split}

so, for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small, we can take
R>2M
suitably large so that we can estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}&r^{p}(L\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{1}{8}\left((1-\epsilon)(1+\beta_{0})^{2}-2|\alpha|\right)r^{p-2}\psi_{0}^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\left(\frac{1}{4}p-\frac{|\alpha|}{2-p}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right)r^{p-1}(L\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p}(L\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}w_{0}^{2}r^{p-2}\left[(X\check{\phi}_{0})^{2}+(T\check{\phi}_{0})^{2}\right]\,d\sigma d\tau+CE_{T}[\phi_{0}](\tau_{1}).\end{split}

(6.6)

Note that

(1+\beta_{0})^{2}-2|\alpha|=1+\beta_{0}^{2}-2|\alpha|+2\beta_{0}>2(1-3|\alpha|)>0,

so for
1-\beta_{0}<p<1+\beta_{0}
and
\epsilon>0
suitably small, the terms on the left-hand side of (
6.6
) are non-negative definite.

To obtain also control of a weighted spacetime integral of
\underline{L}\psi_{0}
, we multiply both sides of (
3.4
) with
-r^{p-2}\underline{L}\psi
to obtain:

\begin{split}0=&\>L\left[2r^{p-2}(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}\right]+\underline{L}\left[\frac{1}{2}D(V_{\alpha}r^{p-2}+D^{\prime}r^{p-3})\psi^{2}+\frac{1}{2}Dr^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]\\
&+(2-p)Dr^{p-3}(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}D\frac{d}{dr}\left(DV_{\alpha}r^{p-2}\right)\psi^{2}+\frac{1}{4}D\frac{d}{dr}\left(Dr^{p-4}\right)|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\\
=&\>DX\left[r^{p-2}(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}D(V_{\alpha}r^{p-2}+D^{\prime}r^{p-3})\psi^{2}-\frac{1}{4}Dr^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]\\
&+DT\left[hr^{p-2}(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+\frac{\tilde{h}}{4}D(V_{\alpha}r^{p-2}+D^{\prime}r^{p-3})\psi^{2}+\frac{\tilde{h}}{4}Dr^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]\\
&+(2-p)Dr^{p-3}(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}D\frac{d}{dr}\left(DV_{\alpha}r^{p-2}\right)\psi^{2}+\frac{1}{4}D\frac{d}{dr}\left(Dr^{p-4}\right)|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}.\end{split}

(6.7)

We integrate the above equality with
\psi
replaced by
\psi_{0}
in spacetime, combine it with (
6.6
) and (
6.5
), using that
\alpha>-\frac{1}{4}
and
1-\beta_{0}<p<1+\beta_{0}
, and we apply (
6.1
) and (
5.3
) to conclude that (
6.3
) holds.
∎

Corollary 6.3
.

Let
1-\beta_{0}<p<1+\beta_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,V_{\alpha},h,R,p)>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}&Dr^{p}(X(r\phi_{0}))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{p-1}\left[(L(r\phi_{0}))^{2}+h_{0}(T\phi_{0})^{2}+\phi_{0}^{2}\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>CE[\phi_{0}](\tau_{1})+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}Dr^{p}(X(r\phi_{0}))^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

(6.8)

Proof.

By (
2.10
), we can estimate

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\check{\phi}_{0}^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq 4\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}r^{2}(DX\check{\phi}_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+2\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r-2M)\check{\phi}_{0}^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C=C(M,V_{\alpha},h,R)>0
such that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(DX\psi_{0})^{2}+(T\psi_{0})^{2}+\psi^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(DX\check{\phi}_{0})^{2}+(T\check{\phi}_{0})^{2}+\check{\phi}_{0}^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

By combining the above equation with (
5.1
), (
6.1
) and (
6.3
) we obtain (
6.8
).
∎

6.0.2
\alpha>0
or
\ell\geq 1
: integrated energy estimates for
\phi_{\leq\ell_{0}}

In this section we consider simultaneously the case
\alpha>0
and the case
\alpha\leq 0
with
\phi
restricted to angular frequencies
\ell\geq 1
. We can then make use of the following positivity property:

\alpha+\ell(\ell+1)>0.

We will additionally split
\phi
as follows:

\phi=\phi_{\leq\ell_{0}}+\phi_{>\ell_{0}},

where
\ell_{0}
will be taken suitably large (depending on
V_{\alpha}
). When considering
\phi_{\leq\ell_{0}}
, we will make use of Proposition
6.1
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
\alpha>0
, or let
\phi=\phi_{\geq 1}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,\alpha,R,\ell_{0})>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}\leq\infty
:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(X\phi_{\leq\ell_{0}})^{2}+(T\phi_{\leq\ell_{0}})^{2}+\phi_{\leq\ell_{0}}^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1}).\end{split}

(6.9)

Proof.

Let
f:[2M,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
be a differentiable function, which will be specified later. We multiply both sides of (
3.4
) with
fZ\psi=fL\psi-f\underline{L}\psi
:

\begin{split}0=&\>-4fL\psi\underline{L}L\psi+4f\underline{L}\psi L\underline{L}\psi+Dr^{-2}fZ\psi\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-fZ\psi D(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi\\
=&\>\underline{L}(-2f(L\psi)^{2})+L(2f(\underline{L}\psi)^{2})+Z\left(-\frac{f}{2}Dr^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}-\frac{f}{2}D(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi^{2}\right)\\
&-D\frac{df}{dr}((L\psi)^{2}+(\underline{L}\psi)^{2})+\frac{1}{2}D\frac{d}{dr}(fDr^{-2})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}D\frac{d}{dr}(f(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1}))\psi^{2}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
=&\>DX\left[f(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+f(L\psi)^{2}-\frac{f}{2}Dr^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}-\frac{f}{2}D(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi^{2}\right]\\
&+DT\left[-f\tilde{h}(L\psi)^{2}+f\tilde{h}D(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+(1-h)\frac{f}{2}\left(r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi^{2}\right)\right]\\
&-D\frac{df}{dr}((L\psi)^{2}+(\underline{L}\psi)^{2})+\frac{1}{2}D\frac{d}{dr}(fD(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1}))\psi^{2}+\frac{1}{2}D\frac{d}{dr}(fDr^{-2})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots),\end{split}

(6.10)

where we use the schematic notation
\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)
to denote divergences with respect to
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, which vanish after integration over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

We take
f\equiv-1
, integrate over
\{r\geq R\}
, where
R>2M
will be chosen suitably large and apply (
5.1
) to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}(\alpha r^{-3}+O_{\infty}(r^{-4}))\psi^{2}+(1+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))r^{-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+(L\psi)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}Dr^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}Dr^{-2}(\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))\psi^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
\leq&\>CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1})+C\eta\sum_{\ell\leq\ell_{0}}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-2}\psi_{\ell}^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau.\end{split}

We will now restrict to
\phi=\phi_{\ell}
and observe that the spacetime integral on the left-hand side above is non-negative definite, using (
2.6
) in combination with
\alpha+\ell(\ell+1)>0
. We rewrite the
\{r=R\}
boundary terms by first considering separately each
\phi_{\ell}
, with
\ell\leq\ell_{0}
: by Young’s inequality with
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small together with (
3.8
), we obtain:

\begin{split}(L\psi_{\ell})^{2}+(\underline{L}\psi_{\ell})^{2}=&\>(L(rw_{\ell}\check{\phi}_{\ell}))^{2}+(\underline{L}(rw_{\ell}\check{\phi}_{\ell}))^{2}\geq\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}D^{2}\left(\frac{d(rw_{\ell})}{dr}\right)^{2}\check{\phi}_{\ell}^{2}+(1-\epsilon^{-1})r^{2}w_{\ell}^{2}\left((L\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+(\underline{L}\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\right)\\
\geq&\>\frac{1-\epsilon}{8}\left[(1+\beta_{\ell})^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right]r^{-2}\psi_{\ell}^{2}-(\epsilon^{-1}-1)r^{2}w_{\ell}^{2}\left((L\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+(\underline{L}\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\right).\end{split}

Note that

\frac{1}{8}(1+\beta_{\ell})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)-\frac{1}{2}\alpha=\frac{1}{4}(1+\beta_{\ell}),

so there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,\alpha,R)>0
such that

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(\alpha+\ell(\ell+1))r^{-3}\psi_{\ell}^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{1-\epsilon}{4}(1+\beta_{\ell})r^{-2}\psi^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
\leq&\>CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1})+C\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}w_{\ell}^{2}(X\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}+w_{\ell}^{2}(T\check{\phi}_{\ell})^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma d\tau.\end{split}

We can estimate the remaining
\{r=R\}
term on the right-hand side above and sum
\ell
from
\ell=0
(if
\alpha>0
) or
\ell=1
(if
\alpha\leq 0
) to
\ell=\ell_{0}
by applying (
6.1
).
∎

Remark 6.1
.

In contrast with the
\alpha<0
,
\ell=0
setting, we are able to obtain via (
6.9
) a local integrated energy estimate with only the energy
E_{T}[\phi]
appearing on the right-hand side of (
6.9
), i.e.

without any growing weights in
r
in front the the terms in the integral defining
E_{T}[\phi]
; see also the discussion in Step 1 of §
4.2
.

Remark 6.2
.

The combination of (
6.1
) with the above proposition provides also a straightforward way of obtaining a local integrated energy (Morawetz) estimate in the setting of (
1.1
) in a way that is uniform for bounded angular frequencies
0\leq\ell\leq\ell_{0}
and does not require any delicate choices of multiplier functions.

6.0.3
\alpha>0
or
\ell\geq 1
: integrated energy estimates for
\phi_{>\ell_{0}}

In this section, we consider the high angular frequency part
\phi_{>\ell_{0}}
, where
\ell_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
will be taken sufficiently large. Since
\ell_{0}
is large, the presence of the
V_{\alpha}
potential in (
3.1
) does not play an important role and we can apply standard arguments from the setting of the geometric wave equation on Schwarzschild without potential (i.e.
V_{\alpha}\equiv 0
) to obtain an integrated local energy decay estimate (see for example §4.11 of
[
DR13
]
). For the sake of completeness, we include them below.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
R>2M
be arbitrarily large. Then there exists a suitably large
\ell_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and a constant
C=C(M,V_{\alpha},h,\ell_{0},R)>0
, such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}\leq\infty
:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(X\phi_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+(T\phi_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+\left(1-3Mr^{-1}\right)^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1}).\end{split}

(6.11)

Proof.

Let
f:[2M,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
be a differentiable function, which will be specified later. We multiply both sides of (
3.4
) with
\frac{D}{2}\frac{df}{dr}\psi
to obtain:

\begin{split}0=&\>-2D\frac{df}{dr}\psi\underline{L}L\psi+\frac{1}{2}D^{2}r^{-2}\frac{df}{dr}\psi\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}r^{-2}\frac{df}{dr}(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi^{2}\\
=&\>\underline{L}\left(-2\frac{df}{dr_{*}}\psi L\psi\right)+\frac{df}{dr_{*}}\underline{L}\psi L\psi-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d^{2}f}{dr_{*}^{2}}L(\psi^{2})-\frac{1}{2}r^{-2}\frac{df}{dr_{*}}\left[(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
=&\>-\frac{D}{2}X\left[-2\frac{df}{dr_{*}}\psi L\psi-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d^{2}f}{dr_{*}^{2}}\psi^{2}\right]+\frac{D}{2}T\left[-2\tilde{h}\frac{df}{dr_{*}}\psi L\psi-\frac{h}{2D}\frac{d^{2}f}{dr_{*}^{2}}\psi^{2}\right]\\
&-\frac{D}{2}r^{-2}D\frac{df}{dr}\left[(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{4}\frac{d^{3}f}{dr^{3}_{*}}\psi^{2}+D\frac{df}{dr}L\psi\underline{L}\psi+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

By combining with (
6.10
), we obtain

\begin{split}0=&\>X\left[f(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+f(L\psi)^{2}-\frac{f}{2}Dr^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}-\frac{f}{2}D(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi^{2}+\frac{df}{dr_{*}}\psi L\psi+\frac{1}{4}\frac{d^{2}f}{dr_{*}^{2}}\psi^{2}\right]\\
&+T\left[-f\tilde{h}(L\psi)^{2}+f\tilde{h}D(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+(1-h)\frac{f}{2}\left(r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi^{2}\right)-\tilde{h}\frac{df}{dr_{*}}\psi L\psi-\frac{h}{4D}\frac{d^{2}f}{dr_{*}^{2}}\psi^{2}\right]\\
&-\frac{df}{dr}(Z\psi)^{2}+\frac{f}{2}\frac{d}{dr}(D(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1}))\psi^{2}+\frac{f}{2}\frac{d}{dr}(Dr^{-2})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

Note that

\frac{d}{dr}(Dr^{-2})=-2r^{-3}(r-3M),

so by taking
f(r)=-r^{-1}(r-3M)
and applying (
2.6
), we can estimate for
\phi_{\geq\ell_{0}}
, with
\ell_{0}
suitably large:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{f}{2}\frac{d}{dr}(D(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1}))\psi_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2}+\frac{f}{2}\frac{d}{dr}(Dr^{-2})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}\,d\sigma\geq c\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-3}\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}\,d\sigma,

for some constant
c>0
depending on
M
,
V_{\alpha}
and
\ell_{0}
. We obtain (
6.11
) after integrating in spacetime, applying (
5.1
) to estimate the boundary term and then applying (
6.10
) with
f(r)=r^{-1}
to control also a spacetime integral of
(Z\psi_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+(T\psi_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}
.
∎

6.1
Non-degenerate (integrated) energy estimates

In this section, we will derive a non-degenerate integrated energy estimate for all
\alpha>-\frac{1}{4}
. We combine first the integrated energy estimates from the previous section to state integrated energy estimates that are valid for all
\alpha>-\frac{1}{4}
, but which degenerate at
r=2M
.

Corollary 6.6

(Degenerate integrated energy estimates)
.

For
R>2M
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},R)>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}\leq\infty
:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}

\displaystyle r(X(r\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}[r^{2}(DX\phi)^{2}+\phi^{2}+(1-3Mr^{-1})^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}+h_{0}(T\phi)^{2}]\,d\sigma drd\tau

(6.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(X(r\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1}),

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}

\displaystyle r(X(r\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left[r^{2}(DX\phi)^{2}+\phi^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}+h_{0}(T\phi)^{2}\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau

(6.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(X(r\phi))^{2}+r(X(rT\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1})+CME_{T}[T\phi](\tau_{1})

Proof.

We consider separately the cases 1)
\alpha\leq 0
and
\phi=\phi_{0}
, and 2)
\alpha>0
or
\phi=\phi_{\geq 1}
.

In case 1), (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) follow directly from Corollary
6.3
with
p=1
. In fact, (
6.12
) holds without a degeneracy at
r=3M
.

In case 2), we need to complement the estimates Proposition
6.4
and
6.5
with additional
r
-weighted estimates in the region
r\geq R
, with
R>3M
arbitrarily large. We therefore integrate (
6.4
) with
p=1
and combine it with (
5.1
) to estimate terms without a good sign on
\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}
and Proposition
6.4
and
6.5
, together with a standard averaging argument, to estimate boundary terms at
r=R
and obtain (
6.12
).

To obtain (
6.13
), we apply a standard elliptic estimate in a neighbourhood of
r=3M
: let
\chi
be a smooth cut-off function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
2\frac{3}{4}M\leq r\leq 3\frac{1}{4}M
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\leq 2\frac{1}{2}M
and
r\geq 3\frac{1}{2}M
. By rearranging the terms in (
3.2
) and squaring, we obtain

\chi(X(Dr^{2}X\phi))^{2}+\chi(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi)^{2}+2\chi X(Dr^{2}X\phi)\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi\leq C\chi\left[(T^{2}\phi)^{2}+(T\phi)^{2}+(TX\phi^{2})+\phi^{2}\right].

Integrating both sides on
\Sigma_{\tau}
, where we integrate by parts in the
X
-direction and on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, and applying (
2.8
) gives the following elliptic estimate:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{2\frac{3}{4}\leq r\leq 3\frac{1}{4}M\}}&(X^{2}\phi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}X\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{2}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{2\frac{1}{2}\leq r\leq 3\frac{1}{2}M\}}(XT\phi)^{2}+(T^{2}\phi)^{2}+(T\phi)^{2}+(X\phi)^{2}+\phi^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

(6.14)

By the Killing property of
T
,
[\square_{g_{M}}-V_{\alpha},T]=0
, so (
6.12
) also applies with
\phi
replaced by
T\phi
. Combining this fact with (
6.14
) concludes the proof of (
6.13
).
∎

In order to remove the factors
D
in the (integrated) energies that appear in (
6.12
) and which correspond to degeneracies at
r=2M
, we apply the
red-shift mechanism
, developed in
[
DR09
,
DR13
]
. We first introduce the
non-degenerate energy

E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau)
:

E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau):=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2}(X\phi)^{2}+h\tilde{h}r^{2}(T\phi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}+\phi^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

We will moreover denote
E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi]:=E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](0)
.

Proposition 6.7

(Red-shift estimate)
.

For
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},\delta)>0
such that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\leq(2+\delta)M\}}&\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}\psi^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq(2+\delta)M\}}\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(X(r\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{1})\end{split}

(6.15)

Proof.

We multiply both sides of (
3.4
) with
D^{-2}\underline{L}\psi
to obtain

\begin{split}0=&\>4D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi D^{-1}L\underline{L}\psi-r^{-2}D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi+D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1})\psi\\
=&\>2L\left[\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}\right]+2\frac{dD}{dr}\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}D^{-1}\underline{L}\left[r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+(V_{\alpha}+r^{-1}D^{\prime})\psi^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{2}r^{-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\\
&+\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{dr}\left(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1}\right)\psi^{2}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
=&\>DX\left[\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}\right]+T\left[h\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{h}r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{h}(V_{\alpha}+r^{-1}D^{\prime})\psi^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{4}X\left[r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+(V_{\alpha}+r^{-1}D^{\prime})\psi^{2}\right]\\
&+2\frac{dD}{dr}\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}r^{-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{dr}\left(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1}\right)\psi^{2}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
=&\>X\left[D\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\left(V_{\alpha}+r^{-1}D^{\prime}\right)\psi^{2}\right]+T\left[h\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{h}r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{h}(V_{\alpha}+r^{-1}D^{\prime})\psi^{2}\right]\\
&+2\frac{dD}{dr}\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}r^{-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{dr}\left(V_{\alpha}+D^{\prime}r^{-1}\right)\psi^{2}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

Now, we integrate the above equation in
\{2M\leq r\leq(2+\delta)M\}
, with
\delta>0
suitably small to guarantee
V_{\alpha}+r^{-1}D^{\prime}>0
. All the resulting boundary terms are then non-negative definite. Hence, we obtain for
\kappa=\frac{dD}{dr}|_{r=2M}=\frac{1}{2M}
:

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\leq(2+\delta)M\}}h\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{h}r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{h}(V_{\alpha}+r^{-1}D^{\prime})\psi^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\frac{2}{1+\frac{1}{2}\delta}\kappa\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq(2+\delta)M\}}\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}\psi^{2}\Bigg|_{r=(2+\delta)M}\,d\sigma d\tau+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq(2+\delta)M\}}r^{-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-3}\psi^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\leq(2+\delta)M\}}h\left(D^{-1}\underline{L}\psi\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{h}r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{h}(V_{\alpha}+r^{-1}D^{\prime})\psi^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

(6.16)

We conclude that (
6.15
) holds by combining the above equation with (
5.1
), (
2.10
), (
6.12
) and a standard averaging argument to estimate the
r=(2+\delta)M
boundary terms.
∎

Corollary 6.8

(Non-degenerate integrated energy estimates)
.

For
R>2M
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},R)>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}

\displaystyle r(X(r\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left[r^{2}(X\phi)^{2}+\phi^{2}+(1-3Mr^{-1})^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}+h_{0}(T\phi)^{2}\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau

(6.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(X(r\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{1}),

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}

\displaystyle r(X(r\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left[r^{2}(X\phi)^{2}+\phi^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}+h_{0}(T\phi)^{2}\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau

(6.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(X(r\phi))^{2}+r(X(rT\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{1})+CME_{T}[T\phi](\tau_{1}).

Furthermore, for
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
3M<R_{0}<R_{1}
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},R_{0},R_{1},N)>0
such that

\begin{split}\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq N+1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R_{0}\leq r\leq R_{1}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}X^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\sum_{n\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(X(rT^{n}\phi))^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{T}[T^{n}\phi](\tau_{1}).\end{split}

(6.19)

Proof.

We obtain (
6.17
) and (
6.18
) by combining (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) with (
6.15
).

The inequality (
6.19
) follows by applying (
6.12
), restricted to
R_{0}\leq r\leq R_{1}
and then apply (
6.14
) to express higher-order
X
and
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
derivatives in terms of higher-order
T
derivatives.
∎

Applying the red-shift estimate, we can moreover derive a non-degenerate energy boundedness estimate:

Corollary 6.9

(Non-degenerate energy estimate)
.

There exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha})>0
such that for all
0<\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}

E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{2})\leq CE_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{1}).

(6.20)

Proof.

By adding to both sides of (
6.16
) the spacetime integral
\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}E_{T}[\phi](\tau)\,d\tau
, we obtain that for some
b>0
we have for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
:

E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{2})+b\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}E_{\mathbf{N}}(\tau)\,d\tau\leq C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}E_{T}[\phi](\tau)\,d\tau+E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{1}).

We have that
\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}E_{T}[\phi](\tau)\,d\tau\leq C(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})E_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1})
as a result of (
5.1
), so after: 1) rearranging terms, 2) dividing by
\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}
and 3) taking the limit
\tau_{2}\downarrow\tau_{1}
, we obtain for all
\tau\geq 0
the following inequality: for all
\tau\geq\tau_{1}

\frac{d}{d\tau}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau)+bE(\tau)\leq CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1}),

which is equivalent to

\frac{d}{d\tau}(e^{b(\tau-\tau_{1})}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau))\leq Ce^{b(\tau-\tau_{1})}E_{T}[\phi](\tau_{1})

from which (
6.20
) follows after integrating in
\tau
.
∎

7
Higher-order
r
-weighted energy estimates

In this section, we will derive
r
-weighted energy estimates for weighted higher-order derivatives of
\psi
of the form
(rL)^{k}\psi
, with
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. These will form the main tool for establishing time decay of higher-order
T
-derivatives in the next section.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})\cap C^{1}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}})
be a solution to (
3.1
). Then

\begin{split}0=&\>-4\underline{L}L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi+Dr^{-2}[\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})](rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi-2N[r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
&-\frac{N}{2}Dr^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi+N\sum_{n=0}^{N}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi+N(N-1)\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi.\end{split}

(7.1)

Proof.

We will prove the identity via induction. Note first of all that the
N=0
case follows from (
3.4
). Now suppose (
7.1
) holds. Then we can multiply both sides of the equation by
rD^{-1}
and rearrange some terms to obtain:

\begin{split}0=&\>-4\underline{L}(rD^{-1}L)^{N+1}\psi+r^{-1}[\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})](rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi-2(N+1)[r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})](rD^{-1}L)^{N+1}\psi\\
&-\frac{N}{2}r^{-1}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi+N\sum_{n=0}^{N}O_{\infty}(r^{-1})(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi+N(N-1)\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi.\end{split}

Then, act with
L
on both sides to obtain:

\begin{split}0=&\>-4\underline{L}L(rD^{-1}L)^{N+1}\psi+Dr^{-2}[\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})](rD^{-1}L)^{N+1}\psi-[2(N+1)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})](rD^{-1}L)^{N+1}\psi\\
&-\frac{N+1}{2}Dr^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi+\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi+N\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi.\end{split}

Hence, (
7.1
) holds also with
N
replaced by
N+1
and the induction argument is complete.
∎

Proposition 7.2

(Higher-order
r
-weighted energy estimates)
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})\cap C^{1}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}})
be a solution to (
3.1
). Let
\max\{1-\beta_{0},0\}<p<\min\{1+\beta_{0},2\}
. Then, for
R>3M
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,N,R,\alpha,p)>0
such that

\begin{split}\sum_{0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p}(L(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\psi)^{2}+r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{p-3}((rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&C\sum_{0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p}(L(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\sum_{n\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(XT^{n}\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\sum_{0\leq n\leq N}E_{T}[T^{n}\phi](\tau_{1}).\end{split}

(7.2)

Proof.

Let
\chi:[M,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be a smooth cut-off function, such that
\chi(r)=1
for
r\geq R
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\leq 3M+\frac{R-3M}{2}
.

We multiply both sides of (
7.1
) with
-\chi r^{p}L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi
and apply the Leibniz rule to obtain:

\begin{split}0=&\>2\chi r^{p}\underline{L}((L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2})-D\chi r^{p-2}[\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})](rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
&+2N[r^{p-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})](L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}+\frac{N}{2}D\chi r^{p-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
&+N\sum_{n=0}^{N}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi+N(N-1)\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
=&\>\underline{L}\left[2\chi r^{p}(L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\right]+L\left[\frac{1}{2}D\chi r^{p-2}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi|^{2}+(\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))((rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\right)\right]\\
&+\chi r^{p-1}\left[p+2N+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right](L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(2-p)\chi r^{p-3}(1+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi|^{2}\\
&+\frac{1}{4}(2-p)\chi r^{p-3}(\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))((rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}++\frac{N}{2}D\chi r^{p-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
&+N\sum_{n=0}^{N}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi+N(N-1)\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
&+\textnormal{Err}_{\chi,N}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

(7.3)

where
\textnormal{Err}_{\chi,N+1}
denotes all terms multiplied by derivatives of
\chi
and squares of derivatives of
\phi
up to order
N+1
. We can further rewrite (
7.3
) as follows:

\begin{split}0=&\>DX\left[\frac{1}{4}\chi r^{p-2}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi|^{2}+(\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))((rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\right)-\chi r^{p}(L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\right]\\
&+DT\left[\chi\tilde{h}r^{p}(L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}+\frac{h}{4D}\chi r^{p-2}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi|^{2}+(\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))((rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\right)\right]\\
&+\chi Dr^{p-1}\left[p+2N+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right](L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(2-p)\chi r^{p-3}(1+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi|^{2}\\
&+\frac{1}{4}(2-p)D\chi r^{p-3}(\alpha+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))((rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}+\frac{N}{2}D\chi r^{p-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
&+ND\sum_{n=0}^{N}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi+N(N-1)D\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
&+\textnormal{Err}_{\chi,N+1}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

(7.4)

We will prove (
7.2
) by induction. We first show that (
7.2
) holds for
N=0
. We integrate (
7.4
) with
N=0
. We apply (
2.10
) to estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}&\frac{1}{4}(2-p)D\chi r^{p-3}(\max\{-\alpha,0\}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))((rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\chi r^{p-3}((2-p)^{-1}\max\{-\alpha,0\}+Cr^{-1})r^{p-1}(X(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+&\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\textnormal{Err}_{\chi,N}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}4\chi((2-p)^{-1}\max\{-\alpha,0\}+Cr^{-1})r^{p-1}(L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}+Cr^{p-1}h^{2}(T(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\textnormal{Err}_{\chi,N}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

We can absorb the first term on the very right-hand side above into the spacetime integral of
\chi Dpr^{p-1}(L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}
provided that
R>3M
is chosen suitably large and moreover, when
\alpha<0
,
1-\beta_{0}<p<1+\beta_{0}
. The second term on the very right-hand side, which is only present in the
M>0
case, can be estimated using (
6.12
) when
N=0
. We moreover use (
6.19
) to estimate
\textnormal{Err}_{\chi,N+1}
, so that we are left with (
7.2
) for
N=0
.

Now suppose we have already established (
7.2
) for
N
replaced with
N-1
. We will show that (
7.2
) holds also for
N
. We integrate (
7.4
) again and apply (
2.10
) as above. In this case, however, we estimate the integral of
r^{p-1}h^{2}(T(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}
by observing that

r^{p-1}h^{2}(T(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}\leq Cr^{p-3}(L(rD^{-1})^{N-1}T\psi)^{2}

and applying the induction hypothesis with
\psi
replaced by
T\psi
, using that
[\square_{g},T]=0
by the Killing property of
T
. We moreover estimate

\begin{split}ND&\sum_{n=0}^{N}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi+N(N-1)D\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi\\
\leq&\>\epsilon\chi r^{p-1}(L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}+\epsilon\chi r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi|^{2}+C\chi\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{N}r^{p-3}((rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi)^{2}\\
&+C\chi\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\chi O_{\infty}(r^{p-1})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n}\psi|^{2}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

We can absorb the terms with a factor
\epsilon
into
\chi D(p+2N)r^{p-1}(L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi)^{2}
and we apply the induction hypothesis together with (
2.10
) to absorb also the terms in the sums above.

We are left with estimating the spacetime integral of
\frac{N}{2}D\chi r^{p-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi
. We first apply the Leibniz rule to obtain:

\begin{split}\frac{N}{2}&D\chi r^{p-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi L(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi=-L\left[\frac{N}{4}D\chi r^{p-2}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\right]\\
&+\frac{N}{4}D^{2}\chi r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi|^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N-1}\psi\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{N}\psi.\end{split}

Note that the second term on the right-hand side has a good sign, whereas the third term can be estimated by applying Young’s inequality together with the induction hypothesis.

The boundary integral terms along
\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}
that are generated in the above estimates either have a good sign or can be estimated by using the induction hypothesis.
∎

8
Decay-in-time estimates for time derivatives

In this section, we extract from Proposition
7.2
a hierarchy of
r
-weighted energy estimates, in order to obtain decay in time of (
r
-weighted) energies for
T^{k}\phi
, with
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We will show that the larger
k
is, the stronger the decay is that we can establish.

In the lemma below we show that we can express energies involving
T
derivatives in terms of energies involving
L
and
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
derivatives

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
R>3M
. For all
p\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that there exists a constant
C=C(R)>0
, such that in
\{r\geq R\}
:

\begin{split}\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N}&\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p+1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}LT(rL)^{n_{2}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}+1}(rL)^{n_{2}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

(8.1)

Proof.

We use that
T=L+\underline{L}
to estimate in
\{r\geq R\}
:

\begin{split}r^{p+1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}LT(rD^{-1}L)^{n_{2}}\psi|^{2}\leq&\>Cr^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n_{2}+1}\psi|^{2}+Cr^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}(rD^{-1}L)^{n_{2}}\psi|^{2}\\
&+r^{p+1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}\underline{L}L(rD^{-1}L)^{n_{2}}\psi|^{2}.\end{split}

Then we apply (
7.1
) to estimate further:

r^{p+1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}\underline{L}L(rD^{-1}L)^{n_{2}}\psi|^{2}\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{n_{2}}r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rD^{-1}L)^{k}\psi|^{2}+C\sum_{k=0}^{n_{2}+1}r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}(rD^{-1}L)^{k}\psi|^{2}.

The inequality (
8.1
) then follows by combining the above with (
2.8
).
∎

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then for
R>3M
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=~C(M,h,\alpha,N,R,\delta)~>~0
such that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\begin{split}E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N}\phi](\tau)\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-2N+\delta}\bBigg@{4}[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2N\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3N\\
n_{1}\leq N\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\bBigg@{4}].\end{split}

(8.2)

and moreover,

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT^{N}\psi)\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{1-2N+\delta}\bBigg@{4}[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2N-1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3N-1\\
n_{1}\leq N\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\bBigg@{4}].\end{split}

(8.3)

Proof.

We first consider the case
N=0
. Let
\{\tau_{i}\}
be a dyadic sequence with
\tau_{i}\to\infty
as
i\to\infty
. Then we can apply the integrated energy estimate (
6.18
) to conclude that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i+1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}&r(L\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau)\,d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(L\psi)^{2}+r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{i})+CE_{T}[T\phi](\tau_{i}).\end{split}

(8.4)

Hence, after applying the mean-value theorem, we conclude that there exists a sequence
\{\tau_{i}^{\prime}\}
with
\tau_{i}\leq~\tau_{i}\leq\tau_{i+1}
along which

E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{i}^{\prime})\leq C(1+\tau_{i}^{\prime})^{-1}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(L\psi)^{2}+r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{i})+CE_{T}[T\phi](\tau_{i})\right].

By applying (
6.20
), we then get that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau)\leq C(1+\tau)^{-1}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(L\psi)^{2}+r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](0)+CE_{T}[T\phi](0)\right].

(8.5)

Now let
N=1
and replace
\psi
with
T\psi
. We then proceed to estimate further: let
\{\tau_{i}\}
be a dyadic sequence and
\gamma\geq 0
, then

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau=&\>\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}\cap\{r\leq R+{\tau_{i}}^{\gamma}\}}r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R+{\tau_{i}}^{\gamma}\}}r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau_{i})^{\gamma}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+C(1+\tau_{i})^{-\gamma}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

(8.6)

Subsequently, we apply (
8.1
) with
N=1
to estimate further

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}(L(rL)\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\sum_{k=0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}X\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k+1}\phi|^{2}+r^{2}h\tilde{h}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}T\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

(8.7)

We can therefore integrate (
8.7
) in
\tau
and apply (
7.2
) with
N=1
to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(L(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{T}[\phi](\tau_{i})+CE_{T}[T\phi](\tau_{i})\\
&+C\sum_{k=0}^{1}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}X\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\phi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k+1}\phi|^{2}+r^{2}h\tilde{h}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}T\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.\end{split}

(8.8)

When
k=0
, we can estimate the second integral on the right-hand side of (
8.8
) by applying (
6.17
). When
k>0
, we use the property
[\Omega_{i},\square_{g_{M}}-V_{\alpha}]=0
to directly apply (
6.17
) to
\phi
replaced with
\Omega_{j}\phi
,
j=1,2,3
. Then we can estimate additional angular derivatives in terms of
\Omega_{j}
-derivatives via (
2.5
).

Subsequently, we apply the mean-value theorem and choose
\gamma=0
to obtain the existence of
\tau_{i}\leq\tau_{i}^{\prime}\leq\tau_{i+1}
such that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}^{\prime}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq&\>C(1+\tau_{i})^{-1}\sum_{0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C(1+\tau_{i})^{-1}\left[E_{T}[\phi](\tau_{i})+E_{T}[T\phi](\tau_{i})+E_{T}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi](\tau_{i})\right].\end{split}

Applying (
7.2
) with
p=1
and
N=0
, allows us to infer that in fact for all
\tau\geq 0
, we have that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1}\sum_{0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C(1+\tau)^{-1}\left[E_{T}[\phi](0)+E_{T}[T\phi](0)+E_{T}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi](0)\right].\end{split}

(8.9)

We can use (
8.9
) to estimate the right-hand side of (
8.4
) further and
\phi
is replaced by
T\phi
. We then obtain:

E_{\mathbf{N}}[T\phi](\tau)\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3\\
n_{1}\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\right].

(8.10)

Furthermore, we can split

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R\leq r\leq R+\tau\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R+\tau\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr

and estimate

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R\leq r\leq R+\tau\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr

and, applying (
8.7
) with
R
replaced by
R+\tau
, we can moreover estimate

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R+\tau\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r(L(rL)\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{T}[\phi](\tau)+CE_{T}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi](\tau).

Hence,

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 2\\
n_{1}\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\Bigg]+C(1+\tau)\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

(8.11)

Equipped with (
8.10
) and (
8.9
), we can now make a better choice of
\gamma
to improve the decay rate of
E_{\mathbf{N}}[T\phi](\tau)
: consider (
8.6
) with
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}
to obtain for all
\tau_{i}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{i+1}
:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}&r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C(1+\tau_{i})^{-1+\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[T\phi](\tau_{i})\Bigg]\\
&+C(1+\tau_{i})^{-1-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C(1+\tau_{i})^{-1-\frac{1}{2}}\left[E_{T}[\phi](\tau_{i})+E_{T}[T\phi](\tau_{i})+E_{T}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi](\tau_{i})\right]\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau_{i})^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 2\\
n_{1}\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\right].\end{split}

and hence

\begin{split}E_{\mathbf{N}}[T\phi](\tau)\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{5}{2}}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3\\
n_{1}\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\right].\end{split}

We can now continue this process iteratively by choosing
\gamma=2^{-k}
and obtain:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}

\displaystyle r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2+\frac{1}{2^{k}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr

\displaystyle+C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 2\\
n_{1}\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\Bigg],

\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{N}}[T\phi](\tau)\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-3+\frac{1}{2^{k}}}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3\\
n_{1}\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\right].

Given
\delta>0
, we can therefore take
k
suitably charge to obtain (
8.2
) for
N=1
and (
8.3
) follows by additionally applying (
8.11
). Then, we apply (
8.11
) to obtain moreover (
8.3
) for
N=1
.

The general
N>1
case proceeds inductively. We further estimate the first term on the right-hand side above after replacing
\phi
with
T\phi
. In general, we split

\begin{split}\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq N-1}&\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{1\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq N-1}(1+\tau_{i})^{\gamma}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
&+C\sum_{1\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq N}(1+\tau_{i})^{-\gamma}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\end{split}

(8.12)

after applying (
8.1
). As we pass from
N
to
N+1
, we apply the mean-value theorem an additional time and then optimize
\gamma
as above in order to increase the decay rates in (
8.2
) and (
8.3
) by 2.
∎

When
\alpha\geq 0
, we will moreover make use of the following stronger decay estimates:

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\alpha\geq 0
or let
\alpha<0
and
\phi=\phi_{\geq 1}
. Then for
R>3M
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},N,R)>0
such that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\begin{split}E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N}\phi](\tau)\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2-2N}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2N+1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3N+1\\
n_{1}\leq N\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\Bigg].\end{split}

(8.13)

and moreover,

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT^{N}\psi)\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2N}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2N\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3N\\
n_{1}\leq N\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\phi](0)\Bigg].\end{split}

(8.14)

Proof.

As in the general
\alpha
case, we have that (
8.5
) holds. When
\alpha>0
or
\phi=\phi_{\geq 1}
, we moreover have that (
7.2
) holds for
0<p\leq 2
. Taking
p=2
and
N=0
, we can therefore further estimate:

\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(L\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(L\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau_{i}).

Applying the mean-value theorem, we can therefore improve (
8.5
) to obtain:

E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](\tau)\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(L\psi)^{2}+r(LT\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+E_{\mathbf{N}}[\phi](0)+E_{T}[T\phi](0)+E_{T}[T^{2}\phi](0)\right],

which implies (
8.13
) with
N=0
. We moreover obtain from (
7.2
) with
p=2
that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(L\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\left[\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(L\psi)^{2}\,d\sigma dr+E_{T}[\phi](0)\right],

which implies (
8.14
) with
N=0
.

To obtain the estimates with
N\geq 1
, we then proceed as in the proof of Proposition
8.2
, by repeatedly applying (
8.1
). When
N=1
, for example, we use (
8.8
) to apply the mean-value theorem once more and increase the decay rate of
E_{\mathbf{N}}[T\phi](\tau)
from
-2
to
-3
. Applying (
7.2
) with
N=1
together with the mean-value theorem again then results in a decay rate of
-4
.

Similarly, (
8.7
), together with (
7.2
) with
N=1
and
p=1
and
p=2
allows us to obtain (
8.14
) with
N=1
. The general
N
case then follows in a straightforward manner.
∎

Remark 8.1
.

In Propositions
8.2
and
8.3
there is a loss of total number of derivatives on the right-hand side compared to the left-hand side. This is explained by the loss of derivatives in the integrated energy estimate in (
6.13
) due the presence of trapped null geodesics.

9
Time-inversion theory

In this section, we will show that, given a solution
\phi
to (
3.1
) that arises from appropriate initial data, we can construct initial data for (
3.1
) that lead to a solution
\widetilde{\phi}
satisfying the property that
T\widetilde{\phi}=\phi
. We will refer to
\widetilde{\phi}
as the
time integral
of
\phi
(see the statement of Proposition
9.9
for a precise definition). Formally,

\widetilde{\phi}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)=-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\phi(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau^{\prime},

but since we have not yet established integrability of
\phi
in
\tau
, we will instead construct
\widetilde{\phi}
from initial data by considering an appropriate elliptic problem (and afterwards justify the above integral).

First, we develop the necessary elliptic theory in Section
9.1
, which we then apply in Section
9.2
to construct the time integral initial data.

9.1
Elliptic theory

We introduce the differential operator
\mathcal{L}
:

\mathcal{L}u:=X(Dr^{2}Xu)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u-r^{2}V_{\alpha}u.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
u\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\Sigma)
. Let
-\beta_{0}<q<\beta_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,V_{\alpha},q,h)>0
, such that

\int_{\Sigma}r^{q}\left[r^{2}(Xu)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u|^{2}+u^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{q}(\mathcal{L}u)^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(9.1)

Proof.

We split
u=u_{\leq\ell_{0}-1}+u_{\geq\ell_{0}}
, where
\ell_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
will be chosen suitably large.

u_{\leq\ell_{0}-1}
:

Consider first
u_{\ell}
, with
0\leq\ell\leq\ell_{0}-1
. The constants appearing in the estimates for
u_{\ell}
with
\ell\leq\ell_{0}-1
below, will depend on
\ell_{0}
.

We consider the weight functions
w_{\ell}
from Lemma
3.3
and define:

\check{u}_{\ell}:=w_{\ell}^{-1}u_{\ell}.

Then

\begin{split}w_{\ell}\mathcal{L}u_{\ell}=w_{\ell}\mathcal{L}(w_{\ell}\check{u}_{\ell})=&\>w_{\ell}X(Dr^{2}X(w_{\ell}\check{u}_{\ell}))-[\ell(\ell+1)+r^{2}V_{\alpha}]w_{\ell}^{2}\check{u}_{\ell}\\
=&\>w_{\ell}^{2}X(Dr^{2}X\check{u}_{\ell})+2\frac{dw_{\ell}}{dr}X\check{u}_{\ell}+\check{u}_{\ell}w_{\ell}\left[\frac{d}{dr}\left(Dr^{2}\frac{dw_{\ell}}{dr}\right)-r^{2}V_{\alpha}w_{\ell}\right]\\
=&\>X(Dr^{2}w_{\ell}^{2}X\check{u}_{\ell}),\end{split}

where we applied (
3.7
) to arrive at the last equality.

Then it follows from multiple applications of the Leibniz rule that:

\begin{split}r^{-q}X\check{u}_{\ell}\cdot w_{\ell}\mathcal{L}u_{\ell}=&\>r^{-q}X\check{u}_{\ell}Dr^{2}w_{\ell}^{2}X^{2}\check{u}_{\ell}+(Dr^{2}w_{\ell}^{2})^{\prime}r^{-q}(X\check{u}_{\ell})^{2}\\
=&\>X\left(\frac{1}{2}Dr^{2-q}w_{\ell}^{2}(X\check{u}_{\ell})^{2}\right)+\left[r-M+\frac{q}{2}(r-2M)+w_{\ell}^{-1}\frac{dw_{\ell}}{dr}r(r-2M)\right]r^{-q}w_{\ell}^{2}(X\check{u}_{\ell})^{2}.\end{split}

We can moreover apply (
3.8
) to estimate

\left|w_{\ell}^{-1}\frac{dw_{\ell}}{dr}-\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{\ell}-1)r^{-1}\right|\leq C(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}+r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}).

We apply a weighted Young’s inequality to estimate

\left|r^{-q}X\check{u}_{\ell}\cdot w_{\ell}\mathcal{L}u_{\ell}\right|\leq\epsilon r^{1-q}w_{\ell}^{2}(X\check{u}_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{1}{4\epsilon}r^{-1-q}(\mathcal{L}u_{\ell})^{2}.

We first take
q>q_{\ell_{0}}
, with
q_{\ell_{0}}
suitably large, so that in
\{r\leq R\}
, with
3M<R<\infty
arbitrarily large,

r-M+\frac{q_{\ell_{0}}}{2}(r-2M)+w_{\ell}^{-1}\frac{dw_{\ell}}{dr}r(r-2M)>0.

Then we integrate over
\Sigma\cap\{r\leq R\}
to obtain

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(X\check{u}_{\ell})^{2}\Big|_{r=R}\,d\sigma+\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq R\}}(X\check{u}_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq R\}}(\mathcal{L}u_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(9.2)

Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrarily small. We then consider the region
\{r\geq R\}
, with
R>3M
suitably large so that

r-M+\frac{q}{2}(r-2M)+w_{\ell}^{-1}\frac{dw_{\ell}}{dr}>\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}q+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}-\epsilon\right)r.

Given any
q>-1-\beta_{\ell}
, we can therefore take
\epsilon>0
suitably small, integrate over
\Sigma
and apply (
9.2
) to obtain

\int_{\Sigma}r^{\beta_{\ell}-q}(X\check{u}_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{-1-q}(\mathcal{L}u_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(9.3)

By restricting further to
-1-\beta_{\ell}<q<-1+\beta_{\ell}
and applying (
2.10
) together with compactness of the support of
u_{\ell}
, we obtain

\int_{\Sigma}r^{\beta_{\ell}-q-2}\check{u}_{\ell}^{2}+r^{\beta_{\ell}-q}(X\check{u}_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{-1-q}(\mathcal{L}u_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr

and hence,

\int_{\Sigma}r^{-q-1}{u}_{\ell}^{2}+r^{-q+1}(X{u}_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{-q-1}(\mathcal{L}u_{\ell})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

u_{\geq\ell_{0}}
:

Consider now
u_{\geq\ell_{0}}
. We have that

(\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}=(X(Dr^{2}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}))^{2}+((\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-r^{2}V_{\alpha})(u_{\geq\ell_{0}}))^{2}+2X(Dr^{2}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-r^{2}V_{\alpha})(u_{\geq\ell_{0}}).

Let
-1-\beta_{0}<q<-1+\beta_{0}
. Then we integrate by parts to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma}2r^{-q}X(Dr^{2}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-r^{2}V_{\alpha})(u_{\geq\ell_{0}})\,d\sigma dr=&\>\int_{\Sigma}X(2r^{-q}Dr^{2}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-r^{2}V_{\alpha})(u_{\geq\ell_{0}}))\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\int_{\Sigma}2Dr^{2-q}(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+r^{2}V_{\alpha}(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2})\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\int_{\Sigma}2qDr^{1-q}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-r^{2}V_{\alpha})(u_{\geq\ell_{0}})\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\int_{\Sigma}2Dr^{2-q}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{2}V_{\alpha})u_{\geq\ell_{0}}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

We apply Young’s inequality to estimate:

\begin{split}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}&\left[2qDr^{1-q}(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-r^{2}V_{\alpha})u_{\geq\ell_{0}}+2Dr^{2-q}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{2}V_{\alpha})u_{\geq\ell_{0}}\right]\\
\leq&\>2(Cr^{-2}+q^{2})Dr^{2-q}(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}Dr^{-q}((\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-r^{2}V_{\alpha})(u_{\geq\ell_{0}}))^{2}+\frac{1}{2}Dr^{-q}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2},\end{split}

for some suitably large
C>0
.

We apply (
2.6
) to infer that:

\int_{\Sigma}2(Cr^{-2}+q^{2})Dr^{2-q}(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq\int_{\Sigma}2(\ell_{0}(\ell_{0}+1))^{-1}(Cr^{-2}+q^{2})Dr^{2-q}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr

and moreover

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+r^{2}V_{\alpha}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2}\,d\sigma\geq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ell_{0}(\ell_{0}+1)+r^{2}V_{\alpha})u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2}\,d\sigma,

so for
\ell_{0}>0
suitably large depending on
q
and
V_{\alpha}
, we can absorb all unsigned terms in the integral of
r^{-q}(\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}
over
\Sigma
to obtain: for all
-1-\beta_{0}<q<-1+\beta_{0}
,

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma}&r^{-q}(X(Dr^{2}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}))^{2}+r^{2-q}D|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+r^{2-q}D(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+r^{-q}(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+r^{-q}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2}\,d\sigma d\sigma\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma}r^{-q}(\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

(9.4)

In order to remover the degenerate factors
D
that appear in front of the
(Xu_{\geq L})^{2}
term, we additionally consider:

\begin{split}r\chi Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}=&\>X\left(\frac{1}{2}Dr^{3}\chi(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}r\left((Dr^{2})^{\prime}-Dr\right)\chi(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}X(r\chi|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+\chi r^{3}V_{\alpha}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2})\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\chi(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+\frac{d}{dr}(r^{3}V_{\alpha})u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2})-\frac{1}{2}Dr^{3}\frac{d\chi}{dr}(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+r\frac{d\chi}{dr}(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+r^{2}V_{\alpha}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2})\\
&+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots),\end{split}

with
\chi:[2M,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
a smooth cut-off function, such that
\chi=1
for
r\leq r_{0}
and
\chi=0
for
r>r_{1}
, where
2M<r_{0}<r_{1}<\infty
. Note first of all that
(Dr^{2})^{\prime}-Dr=r
and

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+\frac{d}{dr}(r^{3}V_{\alpha})u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2}\,d\sigma\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2}\,d\sigma

by (
2.6
) and
\ell_{0}
suitably large. Furthermore, we can apply Young’s inequality to estimate:

|r\chi Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|\leq r^{2}(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+(\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}.

We now integrate
r\chi Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}}\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}
over
\Sigma
and apply the above estimates to obtain

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}r^{2}(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}+u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{1}\}}(\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}\}}(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

Finally, we combine the above equation with (
9.4
) to control (in particular):

\int_{\Sigma}r^{2-q}(Xu_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}+r^{-q}u_{\geq\ell_{0}}^{2}\,d\sigma d\sigma\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{-q}(\mathcal{L}u_{\geq\ell_{0}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

By combining all the above estimates and replacing
q
with
-(q-1)
everywhere above, we conclude that (
9.1
) must hold.
∎

Lemma 9.2
.

The following identity holds:

\left[\mathcal{L}+ND^{\prime}r^{2}X\right](rX)^{N}u=(rX)^{N}\mathcal{L}u+N\sum_{n=0}^{N}O_{\infty}(r^{-1})(rX)^{n}u.

(9.5)

Proof.

We will prove (
9.5
