) by induction. Note that (
9.5
) hold for
N=0
by definition of
\mathcal{L}
. Now we suppose as an induction hypothesis that (
9.5
) holds for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We will show that (
9.5
) must then also hold with
N
replaced by
N+1
.

One can verify first of all that

\begin{split}X(Dr^{2}X(rX)^{N+1}u)=&\>(rX+2)X(Dr^{2}X(rX)^{N}u)-(Dr^{2})^{\prime}X(rX)^{N+1}u-(Dr^{2})^{\prime\prime}(rX)^{N+1}u\\
=&\>rX(Dr^{2}X(rX)^{N}u)-D^{\prime}r^{2}X(rX)^{N+1}u\end{split}

Hence, for
M>0
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}(rX)^{N+1}u=&\>X(Dr^{2}X(rX)^{N+1}u)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rX)^{N+1}u-r^{2}V_{\alpha}(rX)^{N+1}u\\
=&\>rX\mathcal{L}(rX)^{N}u+rX(r^{2}V_{\alpha})(rX)^{N}u-D^{\prime}r^{2}X(rX)^{N+1}u\\
=&\>(rX)^{N+1}\mathcal{L}u-(N+1)D^{\prime}r^{2}X(rX)^{N+1}u+\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-1})(rX)^{n}u,\end{split}

where we applied the induction hypothesis to arrive at the last equality. We then conclude (
9.5
) with
N
replaced by
N+1
by rearranging terms.
∎

We now obtain an analogue of Proposition
9.1
with higher-order angular derivatives, as well as an analogue featuring the left-hand side of (
9.5
), the latter of which will be important when we consider higher-order derivatives in the
X
-direction.

We introduce first the space:

C_{c,*}^{\infty}(\Sigma)=\left\{u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)\,|\,u|_{r=2M}\equiv 0\right\}.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
u\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\Sigma)
. Let
-\beta_{0}<q<\beta_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,\alpha,q,h,N)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{\Sigma}r^{q}\left[r^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}Xu|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k+1}u|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}u|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{\Sigma}r^{q}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\mathcal{L}u|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

(9.6)

Let
u\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\Sigma)
if
N\geq 0
and
u\in C^{\infty}_{c,*}(\Sigma)
if
N<0
. Let
-\beta_{0}<q<\beta_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,V_{\alpha},q,h,N)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma}&r^{q}\left[D^{2}r^{2}(X(rXu))^{2}+r^{2}(Xu)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma}r^{q}((\mathcal{L}-ND^{\prime}r^{2}X)u)^{2}+|N|r^{q}(u^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u|^{2}+D^{2}r^{2}(Xu)^{2})\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

(9.7)

Proof.

The estimate (
9.6
) follows immediately applying (
9.1
) with
\phi
replaced by
\Omega^{I}\phi
, where
|I|\leq k
and applying (
2.5
).

To obtain (
9.7
), we first consider for
q\in\mathbb{R}
and
j\in\{0,1\}
:

\begin{split}ND^{j}r^{q+1}Xu(\mathcal{L}u+ND^{\prime}r^{2}Xu)=&\>X\left(\frac{N}{2}D^{j+1}r^{3+q}(Xu)^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}D^{j}r^{q+1}\left[N(2N+1-j)D^{\prime}r-N(q-1)D\right](Xu)^{2}\\
&-\frac{N}{2}X\left(r^{q+1}D^{j}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u|^{2}+D^{j}r^{3+q}V_{\alpha}u^{2}\right)+\frac{N}{2}((q+1)D^{j}+jD^{\prime}r)r^{q}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u|^{2}\\
&+\frac{N}{2}\left(D^{j}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{q+3}V_{\alpha})+jD^{\prime}r\right)u^{2}+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

(9.8)

We have that
N(2N+1-j)>0
for all
N\neq 0
. Furthermore, integrating (
9.8
) along
\Sigma
, we only obtain a boundary term at
r=2M
with a bad sign if
N<0
and
j=0
, in which case we appeal to the assumption that
u\in C^{\infty}_{c,*}(\Sigma)
to conclude that this boundary term actually vanishes.

By taking
j=1
, we can therefore estimate:

\int_{\Sigma}Dr^{q+2}(Xu)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{q}((\mathcal{L}-ND^{\prime}r^{2}X)u)^{2}+|N|D^{2}r^{q+2}(Xu)^{2}+|N|r^{q}(u^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u|^{2})\,d\sigma dr.

Now, we take
j=0
and we apply the above estimate to remove the
D
factor on the left-hand side:

\int_{\Sigma}r^{q+2}(Xu)^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma}r^{q}((\mathcal{L}-ND^{\prime}r^{2}X)u)^{2}+|N|D^{2}r^{q+2}(Xu)^{2}+|N|r^{q}(u^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}u|^{2})\,d\sigma dr.

Finally, in order to control also the integral of
D^{2}r^{q+2}(X(rXu))^{2}
, we repeat the elliptic estimate for
u_{\geq\ell_{0}}
in the proof of Proposition
9.1
with
\mathcal{L}u+ND^{\prime}r^{2}Xu
replacing
\mathcal{L}
and without the restriction to
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
, and we use that the additional term
ND^{\prime}r^{2}Xu
contributes as a lower-order term the elliptic estimate.
∎

In order to define in a clean way the relevant Hilbert spaces on which
\mathcal{L}
is well-defined, we will modify
\mathcal{L}
as follows: let
q\in\mathbb{R}
and
N\in\mathbb{Z}
then

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q,N}v:=

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{q}{2}}\mathcal{L}(r^{-\frac{q}{2}}v)+Nr^{\frac{q}{2}}D^{\prime}r^{2}X(r^{-\frac{q}{2}}v).

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We introduce the Hilbert spaces
\mathbf{H}_{n}
as completions of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
under the norms
||\cdot||_{n}
defined as follows:

||u||^{2}_{n}:=\sum_{0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\int_{\Sigma}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}u|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

We denote with
D_{n}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N})
the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
with respect to the norm
||\cdot||_{n}+||\mathcal{L}_{q,N}(\cdot)||_{n}
. Then

\mathcal{L}_{q,N}:D_{n}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N})\to\mathbf{H}_{n}

is a closed, densely-defined linear operator.

Corollary 9.4
.

Let
v\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\Sigma)
and let
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
-\beta_{0}<q<\beta_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,V_{\alpha},q,n_{1},n_{2},h)>0
, such that:

||D\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}v||_{2}^{2}+||\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}v||_{1}^{2}\leq C||\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}\mathcal{L}_{q,\pm n_{2}}(rX)^{n_{2}}v||^{2}_{0}+Cn_{2}||\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}v||_{0}^{2}+Cn_{2}||D\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}v||_{1}^{2}

(9.9)

where to be able to take a minus sign in
\mathcal{L}_{q,\pm n_{2}}
, we need to assume
v\in C^{\infty}_{c,*}(\Sigma)
.

Proof.

Let
u=r^{-\frac{q}{2}}v
when
M>0
. Then (
9.9
) follows immediately from Proposition
9.1
and Proposition
9.3
.
∎

We will construct
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{-1}
as an operator acting on
\mathbf{H}_{n}
, with arbitrarily large
n\in N_{0}
, by first considering the formal adjoint of
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}
with respect to
\mathbf{H}_{0}
.

Let

\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{*}:\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{*}_{q,N})\to\mathbf{H}_{0}

be the adjoint operator of
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}
, with
w\in\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{*}_{q,N})
if and only if there exists a unique
f\in\mathbf{H}_{0}
such that for all
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)

\langle\mathcal{L}_{q,N}u,v\rangle_{L^{2}([2M,\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}=\langle u,f\rangle_{L^{2}([2M,\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}.

Then
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{*}v=f
.

We introduce moreover the operator
\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}_{q,N}:=\mathcal{L}_{-q,-N}
with domain
\mathcal{D}_{n}(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}_{q,N})
defined as the closure of the space
C_{*,c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
with respect to the norm
||\cdot||_{n}+||\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}_{q,N}(\cdot)||_{n}
.

Lemma 9.5
.

We have that
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{*}=\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}}
with
\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{*}_{q,N})=\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}})
.

Proof.

We will show that
(\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}})^{*}=\mathcal{L}_{q,N}
and
\mathcal{D}_{0}((\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}})^{*})=\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N})
. It is easy to verify by definition of
\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}_{q,N}
and integration by parts that for all
u\in C_{c,*}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
and
v\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
:

\langle\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}}u,v\rangle_{L^{2}([2M,\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}=\langle u,\mathcal{L}_{q,N}v\rangle_{L^{2}([2M,\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}.

(9.10)

Since
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}
and
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}}
are both closed operators, the identity (
9.10
) holds more generally for
u\in\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}_{q,N})
and
v\in\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N})
. It follows that
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}=(\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}})^{*}
as operators acting on
\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N})
, with
\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N})\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{0}((\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}})^{*})
.

It remains to show that
\mathcal{D}_{0}((\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}})^{*})\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N})
. Observe that
\mathbf{H}_{0}=L^{2}([2M,\infty)_{r}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
and the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
is
L^{2}([2M,\infty)_{r}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
with respect to
||\cdot||_{0}
. Let
v\in\mathcal{D}_{0}((\mathcal{L}_{q,N}^{{\dagger}})^{*})
, then by
(
9.10
)
,
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}v\in\mathbf{H}_{0}
exists in a weak sense. Hence, by the above observations, both
v
and
\mathcal{L}_{q,N}v
lie in the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
with respect to
||\cdot||_{0}
and therefore
v\in\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,N})
.
∎

Proposition 9.6
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
f\in\mathbf{H}_{N}
. Let
-\beta_{0}<q<\beta_{0}
. Then there exists a unique
v\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}
such that
\mathcal{L}_{q,0}v=f
.

Proof.

Suppose
N=0
. Then it follows from Corollary
9.4
with
n_{1}=n_{2}=0
that
\ker\mathcal{L}_{q,0}=\{0\}
. We will establish bijectivity by showing that
\textnormal{Ran}\>\mathcal{L}_{q,0}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
.

Let
w\in(\textnormal{Ran}\>\mathcal{L}_{q,0})^{\perp}
and
u\in C^{\infty}_{c,*}(\Sigma)
. Then we have that

0=\langle w,\mathcal{L}_{q,0}u\rangle=\langle\mathcal{L}^{*}_{q,0}w,u\rangle,

so it follows that
w\in\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,0}^{*})
and
w\in\ker\mathcal{L}_{q,0}^{*}
. Using now that by Lemma
9.5
,
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{q,0}=\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}_{q,0}=\mathcal{L}_{-q,0}
, we can conclude from Corollary
9.4
with
n_{1}=n_{2}=0
that
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{q,0}
is injective, so
\ker\mathcal{L}_{q}^{*}=\{0\}
and
w=0
. Hence,
(\textnormal{Ran}\>\mathcal{L}_{q,0})^{\perp}=\{0\}
, so
\textnormal{Ran}\>\mathcal{L}_{q,0}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
.

So, for
f\in\mathbf{H}_{0}
there exists a unique
v\in\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{q,0})
such that
\mathcal{L}_{q,0}v=f
. By applying Corollary
9.4
once more, we in fact have that
v\in\mathbf{H}_{1}
and
Dv\in\mathbf{H}_{2}
.

Now suppose
N=1
. Then we need to show that in fact we have the following improved regularity statement:
v\in\mathbf{H}_{2}
. First of all, from the above argument, together with Corollary
9.4
for
n_{1}=1
and
n_{2}=0
, it follows that
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}v\in\mathbf{H}_{1}
.

By (
9.5
), we have that:

\mathcal{L}_{q,1}(rXv)=rX\mathcal{L}_{q,0}v+r^{\frac{q}{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{1}O_{\infty}(r^{-1})(rX)^{n}(r^{-\frac{q}{2}}v),

where the right-hand side is an element of
\mathbf{H}_{0}
for
f=\mathcal{L}v\in\mathbf{H}_{1}
, since we already established that
v\in\mathbf{H}_{1}
.

We can therefore conclude that
rXv\in\mathbf{H}_{1}
, if we can establish bijectivity of
\mathcal{L}_{q,1}
and apply Corollary
9.4
with
n_{1}=0
and
n_{2}=1
. Bijectivity in turn follows as above, by replacing
\mathcal{L}_{q,0}
with
\mathcal{L}_{q,1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{q,0}^{*}
with
\mathcal{L}_{q,1}^{*}
.

The general
N>0
case follows in the same manner via an induction argument, where we establish first that
v\in\mathbf{H}_{N}
in order to conclude that
v\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}
.
∎

From the above, we can immediately conclude the following properties for solutions to the equation
\mathcal{L}u=F
:

Corollary 9.7
.

Let
-\beta_{0}<q<\beta_{0}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
F:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
such that
r^{\frac{q}{2}}F\in\mathbf{H}_{N}
. Then there exists a unique
u:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
with
r^{\frac{q}{2}}u\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}u=

\displaystyle\>F,

\displaystyle||r^{\frac{q}{2}}u||_{\mathbf{H}_{N+1}}\leq

\displaystyle\>C||r^{\frac{q}{2}}F||_{\mathbf{H}_{N+1}},

with
C=C(V_{\alpha},M,N,q)>0
a constant.

We obtain stronger estimates when restricting
\mathcal{L}
to functions satisfiying
u=u_{\geq 1}
. In that case, it follows immediately that we can take
\beta_{1}
to replace the role of
\beta_{0}
in the proof of Proposition
9.1
and obtain:

Corollary 9.8
.

Let
-\beta_{1}<q<\beta_{1}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
F:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
such that
r^{\frac{q}{2}}F_{\geq 1}\in\mathbf{H}_{N}
. Then there exists a unique
u:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
, with
u=u_{\geq 1}
and with
r^{\frac{q}{2}}u_{\geq 1}\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}u=

\displaystyle\>F,

\displaystyle||r^{\frac{q}{2}}u_{\geq 1}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N+1}}\leq

\displaystyle\>C||r^{\frac{q}{2}}F_{\geq 1}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N+1}},

with
C=C(V_{\alpha},M,N,q)>0
a constant.

9.2
Time integrals along
\Sigma_{0}

Let
\phi
be a smooth solution to (
3.1
). Then by (
3.2
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\phi=

\displaystyle\>F[T\phi],\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with}

\displaystyle F[f]:=

\displaystyle\>2r(1-h)X(rf)-\frac{dh}{dr}r^{2}f+r^{2}h\tilde{h}Tf,

for sufficiently regular functions
f
on
\mathcal{R}
. We will denote the projection of
F[f]
to spherical harmonics of degree
\ell
by
F_{\ell}[f]
(see §
2.2
).

In the proposition below, we construct the time integral corresponding to solutions
\phi
to (
3.1
) arising from appropriate initial data.

Proposition 9.9
.

Let
-\beta_{0}<q<\beta_{0}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Consider initial data
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
for (
3.1
), such that:

\displaystyle r^{\frac{q}{2}+1}X(r\phi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in

\displaystyle\>\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{q}{2}}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{q}{2}}T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{N}.

Then there exists a unique solution
T^{-1}\phi
to (
3.1
), such that
T(T^{-1}\phi)=\phi
, with initial data

(T^{-1}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
, such that moreover:

\displaystyle r^{\frac{q}{2}}X(rT^{-1}\phi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in

\displaystyle\>\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{q}{2}}T^{-1}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{q}{2}}T(T^{-1}\phi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1},

and there exists a constant
C=C(V_{\alpha},h,M,N,q)>0
, such that

||r^{\frac{q}{2}}X(rT^{-1}\phi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}+||r^{\frac{q}{2}}T^{-1}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}\leq C||r^{\frac{q}{2}+1}X(r\phi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}+C||r^{\frac{q}{2}}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}+C||r^{\frac{q}{2}}T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}.

We refer to
T^{-1}\phi
as the
time integral
of
\phi
.

Proof.

Given the choice of initial data in the proposition, we can apply Corollary
9.7
with the inhomogeneity
F=F[\phi]|_{\Sigma_{0}}
to obtain a solution
u
to
\mathcal{L}u=F
. We take as initial data for (
3.1
)
(u,\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
, and we denote the resulting solution as
\widetilde{\phi}
. By the choice of
F
and by application of (
3.1
), it follows then that

(T\tilde{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{0}},T^{2}\tilde{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{0}})=(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}}),

so by uniqueness of solutions to (
3.1
), we must have that
T\widetilde{\phi}=\phi
.
∎

Remark 9.1
.

In Corollary
10.11
below, we provide an alternative characterization of
T^{-1}\phi
as an integral in time of
\phi
along curves of constant
r,\theta,\varphi
, which motivates the nomenclature.

In the corollary below, we show that for
\alpha\geq 0
(
\beta_{0}\geq 1
), we can construct multiple time integrals.

Corollary 9.10
.

Let
\beta_{0}\geq 1
. Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
n_{\beta}=\lfloor\frac{\beta_{0}+1}{2}\rfloor\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
0<\delta<\beta_{0}-n_{\beta}
be arbitrarily small. Consider initial data
(\phi|_{\Sigma},T\phi|_{\Sigma})
for (
3.1
), such that:

\displaystyle r^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}+1-\delta}X(r\phi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in

\displaystyle\>\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-\delta}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-\delta}T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{N}.

Then there exists a unique solution
T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi
to (
3.1
), such that
T^{1+n_{\beta}}(T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi)=\phi
, with initial data
(T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi|_{\Sigma},T^{-n_{\beta}}\phi|_{\Sigma})
, such that:

\displaystyle r^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}X(rT^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in

\displaystyle\>\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}

and

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}T(T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C=C(V_{\alpha},h,M,N,\delta)>0
, such that:

\begin{split}||r^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}X(rT^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi)_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}+||r^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}\leq&\>C||r^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}+1-\delta}X(r\phi)_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}+C||r^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-\delta}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}\\
&+C||r^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}-\delta}T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}.\end{split}

We refer to
T^{-k}\phi
, with
k\in\mathbb{N}
, as the
k
-th time integral
of
\phi
.
If we assume additionally that

\displaystyle r^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}+1-\delta}X(r\phi_{\geq 1})|_{\Sigma}\in

\displaystyle\>\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-\delta}\phi_{\geq 1}|_{\Sigma}\in\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-\delta}T\phi_{\geq 1}|_{\Sigma}\in\mathbf{H}_{N},

then we obtain:

\displaystyle r^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}X(rT^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi_{\geq 1})|_{\Sigma}\in

\displaystyle\>\mathbf{H}_{N}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi_{\geq 1}|_{\Sigma}\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}

and

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptr^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}T(T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi_{\geq 1})|_{\Sigma}\in\mathbf{H}_{N+1}.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C=C(V_{\alpha},h,M,N,\delta)>0
, such that:

\begin{split}||r^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}X(rT^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi)_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}+||r^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-n_{\beta}-\delta}T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}\leq&\>C||r^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}+1-\delta}X(r\phi)_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}+C||r^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-\delta}\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}\\
&+C||r^{\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}-\delta}T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}||_{\mathbf{H}_{N}}.\end{split}

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Proposition
9.9
with
q=\beta_{0}-\delta
and
\delta>0
sufficiently small to construct
T^{-1}\phi
. Then we repeat the argument of Proposition
9.9
with the role of
\phi
replaced with
T^{-1}\phi
and
q=\beta_{0}-1-\delta
to construct
T^{-2}\phi
. We can repeat this procedure a total number
n_{\beta}
times to obtain the desired estimates.

The improved estimates for
\phi_{\geq 1}
follow as above, but we apply Corollary
9.8
instead of Corollary
9.7
.
∎

We will moreover need to determine the precise large-
r
behaviour of
T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi_{0}|_{\Sigma}
.
In the proposition below we will assume that
\beta_{0}\neq 2l+1
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
This is equivalent to the assumption that
\alpha\neq l(l+1)
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. This assumption is made for the sake of convenience. It is still possible to obtain the precise large-
r
behaviour when
\beta_{0}=2l+1
for some
l\in\mathbb{N}
, but the proof of Proposition
9.11
would have to be modified slightly due to cancellations appearing in the asymptotics; see Remark
9.2
and §
4.2
.

Proposition 9.11
.

Let
\beta_{0}\neq 2l+1
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Consider smooth initial data for (
3.1
) with corresponding solution
\phi
.

(i)

Let
\beta_{0}<1
. If
\int_{2M}^{\infty}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]\,dr
is finite and
w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]
is sufficiently regular at
r=\infty
with respect to
rX
, then

\mathfrak{I}_{\beta_{0}}[\phi]:=\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}X(rT^{-1}\phi_{0})=-\frac{1-\beta_{0}}{2\beta_{0}}\int_{2M}^{\infty}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi_{0}]\,dr^{\prime}.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C=C(M,\alpha,h)>0
such that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

\displaystyle\left|(rX)^{N}X(rT^{-1}\phi_{0})(0,r)-\mathfrak{I}_{\beta_{0}}[\phi](rX)^{N}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>Cr^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\beta_{0}}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\left(||(r^{2}(rX)^{n}X(r\phi_{0})||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}+||(rX)^{n}\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}+||r(rX)^{n}T\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\right).

(ii)

Let
\beta_{0}>1
. Assume the initial data for (
3.1
) is compactly supported. Then, for
n_{\beta}=\lfloor\frac{\beta_{0}+1}{2}\rfloor
:

\mathfrak{I}_{\beta_{0}}[\phi]:=\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{\frac{1}{2}+(\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}-n_{\beta})}X(rT^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi_{0})=-\frac{(1-\beta_{0})(3-\beta_{0})\ldots(2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{0})}{2n_{\beta}!\beta_{0}(1-\beta_{0})\ldots(n_{\beta}-\beta_{0})}\int_{2M}^{\infty}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi_{0}]\,dr^{\prime}

is finite and generically non-zero. Furthermore, there exists a constant
C=C(M,\alpha,h)>0
such that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

\displaystyle\left|(rX)^{N}X(rT^{-1-n_{\beta}}\phi_{0})(0,r)-\mathfrak{I}_{\beta_{0}}[\phi](rX)^{N}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}+n_{\beta}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>Cr^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}+n_{\beta}}\Biggl(\sum_{n=0}^{n_{\beta}+N}||r^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}(rX)^{n}X(r\phi_{0})||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}

\displaystyle+||r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}(rX)^{n}\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}+||r^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}(rX)^{n}T\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Biggr).

Proof.

We can express:

X(Dr^{2}w_{0}^{2}XT^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0})=w_{0}F_{0}[\phi],

with
F_{0}=\pi_{0}F
.
By the regularity properties established in Proposition
9.9
, it follows that

\displaystyle Dr^{2}w_{0}^{2}XT^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,2M)=

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}T^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle\>0.

Hence, we can integrate in
r
from
r=2M
to obtain the explicit formula:

Dr^{2}w_{0}^{2}XT^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=\int_{2M}^{r}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]\,dr,

from which it follows that

\displaystyle XT^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle\>(Dr^{2})^{-1}w_{0}^{-2}\int_{2M}^{r}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]\,dr^{\prime},

\displaystyle T^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle-\int_{r}^{\infty}(Dr^{2})^{-1}w_{0}^{-2}\int_{2M}^{r}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]\,dr.

By the assumptions we made on our initial data, we moreover have that

\displaystyle|d_{0}|<

\displaystyle\>\infty,

\displaystyle d_{0}:=

\displaystyle\>\int_{2M}^{\infty}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]\,dr^{\prime}.

Suppose first that
\beta_{0}<1
. Then for sufficiently regular
w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]
at
r=\infty
with respect to
rX
:

\displaystyle\int_{2M}^{r}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]\,dr^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>d_{0}D+DO_{\infty}(r^{\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}).

We moreover have that

\displaystyle w_{0}^{-2}=

\displaystyle\>r^{1-\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{1-2\beta_{0}}).

Hence,

\displaystyle XT^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle[d_{0}r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}-\frac{5}{2}})][r^{1-\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{1-2\beta_{0}})],

\displaystyle T^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle-d_{0}\beta_{0}^{-1}r^{-\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}(1+\beta_{0})})+O_{\infty}(r^{-2\beta_{0}}),

so we obtain the following expansions:

\displaystyle rT^{-1}\phi_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle-d_{0}\beta_{0}^{-1}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(1)+O_{\infty}(r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\beta_{0}}),

\displaystyle X(rT^{-1}\phi_{0})(0,r)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\beta_{0}}(1-\beta_{0})d_{0}r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\beta_{0}}),

So

\mathfrak{I}_{\beta_{0}}[\phi]=\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}X(rT^{-1}\phi_{0})=-\frac{1}{2\beta_{0}}(1-\beta_{0})d_{0}

is finite and generically non-zero. The inequalities in (i) follow by expressing the terms appearing in
O_{\infty}(...)
via derivatives of
F_{0}[\phi]
.

Now suppose
\beta_{0}>1
. By the regularity properties established in Corollary
9.10
, it follows that

\displaystyle Dr^{2}w_{0}^{2}XT^{-n}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,2M)=

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}T^{-n}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle\>0,

for all
1\leq n\leq n_{\beta}
. By the compactness assumption on the initial data, we have in particular that

\displaystyle\int_{2M}^{r}w_{0}F_{0}[\phi]\,dr^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>d_{0}+DO_{\infty}(r^{-2}).

We moreover have that

\displaystyle w_{0}^{-2}=

\displaystyle\>r^{1-\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\beta_{0}}).

Hence, using that
\beta_{0}\neq 1
, since
\beta_{0}\neq 2l+1
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

\displaystyle T^{-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle-d_{0}\beta_{0}^{-1}r^{-\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\beta_{0}-1}),

so we obtain:

\displaystyle rT^{-1}\phi_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle-d_{0}\beta_{0}^{-1}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}),

and

\displaystyle w_{0}F_{0}[T^{-1}{\phi}]=

\displaystyle-\beta_{0}^{-1}(1-\beta_{0})d_{0}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}).

We now consider
T^{-2}\check{\phi}
. From the above expressions, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{2M}^{r}w_{0}F_{0}[T^{-1}\phi]\,dr^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>-\beta_{0}^{-1}(1-\beta_{0})d_{0}Dr+D\log rO_{\infty}(r^{-1}),

and hence,

\displaystyle T^{-2}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle-d_{0}\beta_{0}^{-1}r^{-\beta_{0}+1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}),

where we can take
\delta>0
arbitrarily small.

We will establish analogous estimates for
T^{-k}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)
with
2\leq k\leq 1+n_{\beta}
by induction. We take as the inductive assumption:

T^{-k}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=-d_{0}\frac{(1-\beta_{0})(3-\beta_{0})\ldots(2k-3-\beta_{0})}{\beta_{0}(1-\beta_{0})\ldots(k-1-\beta_{0})}\frac{1}{(k-1)!}r^{k-1-\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{k-2-\beta_{0}-\delta}),

with
2\leq k\leq n_{\beta}
.

Note that the above estimate holds for
k=2
, by above. With the induction assumption, we obtain:

\displaystyle rT^{-k}{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle-d_{0}\frac{(1-\beta_{0})(3-\beta_{0})\ldots(2k-3-\beta_{0})}{\beta_{0}(1-\beta_{0})\ldots(k-1-\beta_{0})}\frac{1}{(k-1)!}r^{k-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{k-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}-\delta}),

\displaystyle w_{0}F[T^{-k}{\phi}_{0}]=

\displaystyle\>-d_{0}\frac{(1-\beta_{0})(3-\beta_{0})\ldots(2k-3-\beta_{0})(2k-1-\beta_{0})}{\beta_{0}(1-\beta_{0})\ldots(k-1-\beta_{0})}\frac{1}{(k-1)!}r^{k-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{k-2+\delta}).

Then it follows that

\displaystyle X(T^{-k-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r))=

\displaystyle-d_{0}\frac{(1-\beta_{0})(3-\beta_{0})\ldots(2k-3-\beta_{0})(2k-1-\beta_{0})}{\beta_{0}(1-\beta_{0})\ldots(k-1-\beta_{0})}\frac{1}{k(k-1)!}Dr^{k-1-\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{k-2-\beta_{0}+\delta}),

\displaystyle T^{-k-1}\check{\phi}_{0}(0,r)=

\displaystyle-d_{0}\frac{(1-\beta_{0})(3-\beta_{0})\ldots(2k-3-\beta_{0})(2k-1-\beta_{0})}{\beta_{0}(1-\beta_{0})\ldots(k-1-\beta_{0})(k-\beta_{0})}\frac{1}{k(k-1)!}Dr^{k-\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{k-1-\beta_{0}+\delta})

Hence, the induction assumption holds also with
k
replaced by
k+1
.

We arrive at the inequality in (ii) by estimating the terms in
O_{\infty}(\ldots)
via
F_{0}[\phi]
and its
X
-derivatives.∎

Remark 9.2
.

If
\beta_{0}=2l+1
, for some
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, it can be shown that, given smooth and compactly supported initial data for
\phi
,
T^{-l-2}\phi_{0}
is well-defined and

X(rT^{-l-2}\phi_{0})|_{\Sigma}=D_{l}r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2}).

Since we do not make use of this estimate, we will leave it to the reader to prove this statement and determine the precise form of
D_{l}
.

10
Late-time asymptotics and time-decay estimates

We will now apply Proposition
8.2
with
N=n_{\beta}+1
to the time integral
T^{-n_{\beta}-1}\phi
, where
n_{\beta}=\lfloor\frac{\beta_{0}+1}{2}\rfloor
. We will see that the estimates in Corollary
9.10
do not provide enough regularity for
T^{-n_{\beta}-1}\phi
to conclude that the weighted, higher-order energy norms appearing on the right-hand side of (
8.2
) are finite. Hence,
the time-decay will be limited by the
r
-decay rates of
T^{-n_{\beta}-1}\phi
along
\Sigma_{0}
.

In order to go beyond (almost-)sharp time-decay estimates for
\phi
to the precise late-time asymptotics of
\phi
as
\tau\to\infty
, we will consider the difference

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}=

\displaystyle\>\phi-\Phi,

\displaystyle\Phi(\tau,r)=

\displaystyle\>\Phi_{0}w_{0}(r)(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}},

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}\in

\displaystyle\>\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}.

The goal of this section is to show that the global leading-order behaviour in time of
\phi
is actually determined by
\Phi
. We will show this as follows:

1.

We will establish sufficiently strong decay estimates for
\widehat{\phi}
, which satisfies an inhomogeneous wave equation:

(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\widehat{\phi}=-(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi.

(10.1)

In Lemma
10.1
below, we show that the choice of the function
\Phi
ensures that
(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi
has favourable decay properties in
\tau
and
r
, which will allow us to apply the energy decay estimates of Proposition
8.2
to
\widehat{\phi}
.

2.

We will choose the constant
\Phi_{0}
such that the
(1+n_{\beta})
-th time integral of the difference
\widehat{\phi}
has
better
decay properties in
r
along
\Sigma_{0}
than the
(1+n_{\beta})
-th time integral of
\phi
. This allows us to use Proposition
8.2
to show that
\widehat{\phi}
decays
faster
than
\Phi
, so the late-time behaviour of
\phi
is determined (globally) by
\Phi
.

We define
\Phi^{(k)}
as follows

\Phi^{(k)}(\tau,r):=(-1)^{k}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\tau_{1}}\ldots\int_{\tau_{k-1}}\Phi(\tau_{k},r)\,d\tau_{k}d\tau_{k-1}\ldots d\tau_{1}.

Then
T^{k}\Phi^{(k)}=\Phi
. We will also denote
T^{-k}\Phi=\Phi^{(k)}
.

Furthermore, we can integrate by parts
k-1
times in
\tau
to express:

\Phi^{(k)}(\tau,r)=\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(k-1)!}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}(s-\tau)^{k-1}\Phi(s,r)\,ds

for
k<\beta_{0}+1
.

We denote moreover

T^{-k}\widehat{\phi}=T^{-k}\phi-\Phi^{(k)}.

Lemma 10.1
.

(i)

For all
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
k\leq\beta_{0}+1
, there exists a
C=C(M,V_{\alpha},n_{1},n_{2},k)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\left|(rX)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(k)}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C|\Phi_{0}|r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}-\min\{1,\beta_{0}\}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}+k-n_{2}},

(10.2)

(ii)

Let
\beta_{0}\neq 2l+1
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that
k<\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}
, we can expand

X(r\Phi^{(k+1)})|_{\Sigma_{0}}=\frac{(-1)^{k}2^{k-\beta_{0}}\pi\Gamma(\beta_{0}-k)\Phi_{0}}{k!\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta_{0}+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta_{0}-1-2k}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\beta_{0}-2k}{2\pi}\right)}r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}+k}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}+k})+O_{\infty}(r^{-2}).

(10.3)

Proof.

We can write

(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi=r^{-2}X(Dr^{2}X\Phi)-2(1-h)XT\Phi+h\tilde{h}T^{2}\Phi+\left[-2r+\left(r^{2}h\right)^{\prime}\right]r^{-2}T\Phi-V_{\alpha}\Phi.

If we denote
f(\tau,r)=(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}
, and we use (
3.7
), we can write:

\begin{split}\Phi_{0}^{-1}(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi=&\>w_{0}DX^{2}f-2(1-h)w_{0}XTf+\left[(Dr^{2})^{\prime}+2Dr^{2}w_{0}^{-1}w_{0}^{\prime}\right]r^{-2}w_{0}Xf\\
&+\left[-2(1-h)r^{2}w_{0}^{-1}w_{0}^{\prime}-2r+\left(r^{2}h\right)^{\prime}\right]r^{-2}w_{0}Tf+h\tilde{h}w_{0}T^{2}f.\end{split}

We will need the following properties of the function
f
:

\displaystyle Xf=

\displaystyle\>-(\beta_{0}+1)(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}},

\displaystyle Tf=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{0}+1)((\tau+1+2r)^{-1}+(\tau+1)^{-1})(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>-(\beta_{0}+1)(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}-(\beta_{0}+1)r(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}},

\displaystyle X^{2}f=

\displaystyle\>(\beta_{0}+1)(\beta_{0}+3)(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}},

\displaystyle XTf=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{0}+1)(\beta_{0}+3)(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{0}+1)^{2}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}},

\displaystyle T^{2}f=

\displaystyle\>(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}O_{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}),

\displaystyle w_{0}=

\displaystyle\>r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}),

\displaystyle w_{0}^{\prime}w_{0}^{-1}=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{0}-1)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1-\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-2}),

to obtain

\begin{split}\Phi_{0}^{-1}\square_{g}\Phi=&\>(\beta_{0}+1)(\beta_{0}+3)[r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})](\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}\\
&-(\beta_{0}+1)(\beta_{0}+3)[r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})](\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}\\
&-(\beta_{0}+1)^{2}[r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})](\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}\\
&-(\beta_{0}+1)[(\beta_{0}+1)r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{5}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})](\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}\\
&+(\beta_{0}+1)[(\beta_{0}+1)r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{5}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})](\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}\\
&+(\beta_{0}+1)[(\beta_{0}+1)r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})](\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}\\
&+[r^{-\frac{5}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})+O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{7}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}})](\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}).\end{split}

Note that most of the terms above cancel, so we are left with

\Phi_{0}^{-1}(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi=(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}}O_{\infty}(r^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}-\min\{1,\beta_{0}\}}).

We conclude that the estimates in (i) hold.

We can verify by differentiating in time
\tau
and appealing to standard properties of hypergeometric functions
\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(a,b,c;z\right)
(see for example
[
Olv74
]
) that:

\begin{split}&X(rr^{\frac{-1+\beta_{0}}{2}}w_{0}^{-1}\Phi^{(k+1)})(\tau,r)=\frac{(-1)^{k+1}}{(k+1)!}\frac{\pi(1+\beta_{0})}{2\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2}(\beta_{0}-2k)\right)}r^{\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{0}-1)}(1+2r+\tau)^{-\beta_{0}}\\
\cdot&\Bigg[-\frac{\Gamma(\beta_{0}-k)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3+\beta_{0}}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta_{0}-1-2k}{2}\right)}(2+2r+\tau)^{k}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{0}-1),\beta_{0}-k,\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{0}-1-2k);\frac{\tau+1}{\tau+2r+1}\right)\\
&+\frac{\Gamma(1+k)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta_{0}-1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{5-\beta_{0}-1+k}{2}\right)}(2+2r+\tau)^{\frac{3-\beta_{0}+k}{2}}(1+\tau)^{\frac{3-\beta_{0}+2k}{2}}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}(3+\beta_{0}),1+k,\frac{5-\beta_{0}+k}{2};\frac{\tau+1}{\tau+2r+1}\right)\Bigg].\end{split}

(10.4)

To obtain (ii), we use that

r^{\frac{-1+\beta_{0}}{2}}w_{0}^{-1}=1+O_{\infty}(r^{-\min\{\beta_{0},1\}})

and we evaluate the expression on the right-hand side of (
10.4
) at
\tau=0
and expand the hypergeometric functions in
\frac{1}{r}
.
∎

Remark 10.1
.

If we take
M=0
in the Schwarzschild metric
g_{M}
and we fix
V_{\alpha}=\alpha r^{-2}
, we in fact have that:
(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi=0
.

Remark 10.2
.

If
\beta_{0}=2l+1
for some
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, it can be shown that the following asymptotics hold:

X(r\Phi^{(l+1)})(0,r)=(-1)^{l+1}\frac{l!}{2^{l+1}(2l+1)!}\Phi_{0}r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2}).

We now fix the constant
\Phi_{0}
:

\Phi_{0}=(-1)^{n_{\beta}}n_{\beta}!\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta_{0}+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta_{0}-1-2n_{\beta}}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\beta_{0}-2n_{\beta}}{2\pi}\right)}{2^{n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}}\pi\Gamma(\beta-n_{\beta})}\mathfrak{I}_{\beta}[\phi].

(10.5)

With the choice (
10.5
), it follows immediately that we can obtain improved decay properties in
r
for
T^{-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi}_{0}
compared to Proposition
9.11
, since this choice of
\Phi_{0}
ensures a cancellation between the leading-order terms in
r^{-1}
of
X(rT^{-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi}_{0})
and
X(r\Phi^{(1+n_{\beta})})
:

Corollary 10.2
.

Assume
\beta_{0}\neq 2l+1
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
\Phi_{0}
satisfy (
10.5
) and assume smooth initial data for (
3.1
).

(i)

Let
\beta_{0}<1
. If
\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{2}X(r\phi)|_{\Sigma}<\infty
. Then for all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists a constant
C=~C(M,\alpha,h,N)>~0
such that:

\displaystyle\left|(rX)^{N}X(rT^{-1}\widehat{\phi}_{0})(0,r)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>Cr^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\beta_{0}}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\left(||(r^{2}(rX)^{n}X(r\phi_{0})||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}+||(rX)^{n}\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}+||r(rX)^{n}T\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\right).

(ii)

Let
\beta_{0}>1
. Assume the initial data are compactly supported. Then, for
n_{\beta}=\lfloor\frac{\beta_{0}+1}{2}\rfloor
and for all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,\alpha,h,N)>0
, such that :

\begin{split}&\left|(rX)^{N}X(rT^{-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi}_{0})(0,r)\right|\\
\leq&\>Cr^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}+n_{\beta}}\Biggl(\sum_{n=0}^{n_{\beta}+N}||r^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}(rX)^{n}X(r\phi_{0})||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\\
&+||r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}(rX)^{n}\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}+||r^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}(rX)^{n}T\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Biggr).\end{split}

10.1
Energy decay estimates for
\widehat{\phi}=\phi-\Phi

In this section and in Section
10.2
below, we establish decay-in-time estimates for (
r
-weighted) energies of
\widehat{\phi}
along
\Sigma_{\tau}
, which are necessary for obtaining the
L^{\infty}
decay-in-time estimates in Section
10.3
.

We will first treat separately the cases
\alpha<0
and
\alpha>0
.

10.1.1
\alpha<0

In order to obtain time-decay for
T^{-1}\widehat{\phi}
, which for
\alpha
such that
3\beta_{0}\leq 1
decays too slowly in
r
to apply directly the (inhomogeneous analogues of) the energy decay estimates of Proposition
8.2
, we first introduce
\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
, which we define to be the solution to

(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}=-(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(1)},

arising from the cut-off initial data:

(\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}|_{\Sigma_{0}})=(\chi_{r_{\infty}}T^{-1}\widehat{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{0}},\chi_{r_{\infty}}\widehat{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{0}})

with
r_{\infty}>3M
and
\chi_{r_{\infty}}
a smooth cut-off function such that
\chi_{r_{\infty}}=1
for
r\leq r_{\infty}
and
\chi_{r_{\infty}}=0
for
r\geq 2r_{\infty}
.

By the domain of dependence property of (
3.1
), we have that

T^{k-1}\widehat{\phi}=T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{in}\hskip 10.00002pt\left\{v\leq v|_{\tau=0}(r_{\infty}):=v_{r_{\infty}}\right\}

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

We first prove analogues of the non-degenerate energy boundedness estimate (
6.20
) and the integrated energy estimates in Corollary
6.8
for
\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
, which take into account the inhomogeneity in the equation for
\widehat{\phi}
.

Proposition 10.3
.

Let
\alpha<0
and
3\beta_{0}\leq 1
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},N,\delta)>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}

(i)

\begin{split}E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{2})\leq&\>CE_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{1})+C(1+\tau_{1})^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau_{1})^{-3\beta_{0}-2k+2\delta},\end{split}

(10.6)

(ii)

\begin{split}E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{2})+&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>CE_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{1})+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{k+1}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{1})+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\Phi_{0}^{2}\max\{(1+\tau_{1})^{1-3\beta_{0}-2k+2\delta},(1+\tau_{2})^{1-3\beta_{0}-2k+2\delta}\},\end{split}

(10.7)

(iii)

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R_{0}\leq r\leq R_{1}\}}\Bigg[\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq N}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}X^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}+k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}+1}X^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}+k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}|^{2}\\
&+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}X^{n_{2}+2}T^{n_{3}+k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}|^{2}+h_{0}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}X^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}+1+k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}|^{2}\Bigg]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{n=1}^{N+k}E_{T}[T^{n_{2}}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{1})+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r(LT^{n}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\Phi_{0}^{2}\max\{(1+\tau_{1})^{1-3\beta_{0}-2k+2\delta},(1+\tau_{2})^{1-3\beta_{0}-2k+2\delta}\}.\end{split}

(10.8)

Proof.

Let
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
. We will first prove
(i)
with
E_{T}
instead of
E_{\mathbf{N}}
on the right-hand side. Note that (
5.1
) no longer holds due to the presence of an inhomogeneity in the equation for
\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
. In the estimate for (
5.1
) we instead encounter the additional term:

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2}T\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}\cdot(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(1)}\,d\sigma dr.

After integrating by parts in
\tau
and applying Young’s inequality with appropriate weights depending on
\tau
and
r
and
\epsilon>0
and
\delta>0
arbitrarily small, we can estimate the norm of the above integral by:

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\epsilon(1+\tau)^{-2}r^{1-\delta}(\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}r^{3+\delta}(1+\tau)^{2}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})T\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}}\epsilon(\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}r^{2-2\delta}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

We have that

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}}\epsilon(\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq\epsilon\left[E_{T}[\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{1})+E_{T}[\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{2})\right]

and by (
2.10
), it follows that

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\epsilon(1+\tau)^{-2}r^{1-\delta}(\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\epsilon(1+\tau_{1})^{-1}\sup_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{1-\delta}(X\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

By applying moreover the estimates in
(i)
of Lemma
10.1
, we can also further estimate for
\delta>0
suitably small:

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}r^{3+\delta}(1+\tau)^{2}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})T\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq

\displaystyle\>C\epsilon^{-1}\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau_{1})^{-3\beta_{0}+\delta},

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}}\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}r^{2-2\delta}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\epsilon^{-1}\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau_{1})^{-2-2\beta_{0}}.

We can similarly, replace
\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
by
T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
above and use that

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{-1}r^{3+\delta}(1+\tau)^{2}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})T^{k+1}\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq

\displaystyle\>C\epsilon^{-1}\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau_{1})^{-3\beta_{0}-2k+\delta},

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}}\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}r^{2-2\delta}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})T^{k}\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\epsilon^{-1}\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau_{1})^{-2-2\beta_{0}-2k}.

Hence, for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small, we can estimate

\begin{split}E_{T}[T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{2})\leq&\>(1+\epsilon)E_{T}[T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau_{1})+\epsilon(1+\tau_{1})^{-1}\sup_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{1-\delta}(LT^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\epsilon^{-1}\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau_{1})^{-3\beta_{0}-2k+\delta}.\end{split}

(10.9)

In order to replace
E_{T}
on the left-hand side with
E_{\mathbf{N}}
we proceed as in the derivation of (
6.16
), but due to the presence of an the inhomogeneity, we need to include the following additional term on the right-hand side:

C\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\leq(2+\delta)M\}}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq C\epsilon^{-1}\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau)^{-1-2\beta_{0}}.

Then, we apply the proof of Corollary
6.9
to conclude that (
10.9
) holds with
E_{T}
replaced with
E_{\mathbf{N}}
on both sides of the equation.

In order to arrive at the integrated energy estimates in Corollary
6.8
with
\phi
replaced by
\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
, we similarly have to estimate the following main additional term involving the extra inhomogeneity in the arguments leading to a proof of Corollary
6.8
:

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r|L\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}|\cdot r|(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(1)}|\,d\sigma dr\leq\epsilon\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{-1-\delta}r(L\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{3}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

We estimate the right-hand side as above via
(i)
of Lemma
10.1
, noting that

\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{3}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\epsilon^{-1}\Phi_{0}^{2}\max\{(1+\tau_{2})^{1-3\beta_{0}},(1+\tau_{1})^{1-3\beta_{0}+2\delta}\}

and similarly, for
k\geq 1
:

\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{3}(1+\tau)^{1+\delta}((\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})T^{k}\Phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\epsilon^{-1}\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau_{1})^{1-2k-3\beta_{0}+2\delta}.

By combining (
10.9
) with the above integrated energy estimates, we can remove the supremum over
\tau
appearing on the right hand side of (
10.9
) to conclude that
(i)
holds, and we can also conclude that
(ii)
holds.

Finally,
(iii)
follows from
(ii)
, without loss of
T
-derivatives on the right-hand side by applying elliptic estimates as in the proof of Corollary
6.8
, using that the additional terms involving the inhomogeneity can be bounded straightforwardly by applying
(i)
of Lemma
10.1
again.
∎

In the proposition below we obtain energy decay estimates for
\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
in terms of initial energies for
\widehat{\phi}
.

Proposition 10.4
.

Let
\alpha<0
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},N,\delta)>0
such that for all
0\leq k\leq 1
:

\begin{split}&E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N+1-k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-2N+2k+\delta}r_{\infty}^{\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]\Bigg]+C|\Phi_{0}|^{2}(1+\tau)^{-2-2N-3\beta_{0}+2k+2\delta}\end{split}

(10.10)

and moreover

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT^{N+1-k}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-2N+2k+\delta}r_{\infty}^{\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]\Bigg]+C|\Phi_{0}|^{2}(1+\tau)^{-2N-3\beta_{0}+2k+2\delta}.\end{split}

(10.11)

When
\phi=\phi_{\geq 1}
, we can take the
r
-weight inside the integrals on the right-hand sides above to be
r
rather than
r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}
and we can omit the factor
r_{\infty}^{\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}+\delta}
and the term involving
|\Phi_{0}|^{2}
.

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Proposition
8.2
and we estimate the additional terms present as a result of the inhomogeneity present in the equation for
\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
as in the proof of Proposition
10.3
, via the application of the estimates in
(i)
of Lemma
10.1
, using moreover the energy boundedness and integrated energy estimates of Proposition
10.3
, in the case
3\beta_{0}\leq 1
.

Then we estimate the energies along
\Sigma_{0}
for
T^{k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
in terms of initial energies for
T^{k}(T^{-1}\widehat{\phi})
. The only non-trivial step here is the following, when
3\beta_{0}<1
:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r(L(rL)^{k}T^{l}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr=&\>\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r(L(rL)^{k}\chi_{r_{\infty}}T^{l-1}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq 2r_{\infty}\}}\chi_{r_{\infty}}^{2}r(L(rL)^{k}T^{l-1}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq 2r_{\infty}\}}r|(rX)^{k_{1}}X(\chi_{r_{\infty}})|^{2}((rL)^{k_{2}}T^{l-1}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>Cr_{\infty}^{1-3\beta_{0}+\delta}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq 2r_{\infty}\}}\chi_{r_{\infty}}^{2}r^{3\beta_{0}-\delta}(L(rL)^{k}T^{l-1}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+Cr_{\infty}^{1-3\beta_{0}+\delta}\sum_{k_{2}\leq k}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{R\leq r\leq 2r_{\infty}\}}r^{-2+3\beta_{0}-\delta}((rL)^{k_{2}}T^{l-1}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>Cr_{\infty}^{1-3\beta_{0}+\delta}\left[\sum_{k_{2}\leq k}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{3\beta_{0}-\delta}(L(rL)^{k_{2}}T^{l-1}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{k}E_{T}[T^{k_{1}+l-1}\widehat{\phi}]\right],\end{split}

where we applied (
2.10
) to estimate the
k_{2}=0
term in the sum on the right-hand side of the second inequality and arrive at the last inequality, estimating the boundary terms at
r=R
in terms of the
T
-energy by averaging appropriately around
r=R
.
∎

10.1.2
\alpha>0
and
\beta_{0}\neq 2l+1

If we assume
\alpha>0
, we define
({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1+n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}
to be the solution to (
3.1
) arising from initial data:

((\phi_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1+n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}|_{\Sigma_{0}},T(\phi_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1+n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}|_{\Sigma_{0}})=(\chi_{r_{\infty}}T^{-1-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}|_{\Sigma_{0}},\chi_{r_{\infty}}T^{-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}|_{\Sigma_{0}}).

We emphasize that in contrast with
{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
above,
({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1+n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}
satisfies the
homogeneous
equation (
3.1
), so the energy boundedness and integrated energy decay estimates of Propositions
5.1
and
6.8
apply directly, without the need for an analogue of Proposition
10.3
. Note moreover that, in contrast with the
\alpha<0
case, we have the additional, stronger, energy decay estimates in Proposition
8.3
at our disposal.

Proposition 10.5
.

Let
\alpha>0
and
\beta_{0}\neq 2l+1
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},N,\delta)>0
such that for all
0\leq k\leq 1+n_{\beta}
:

(i)

\begin{split}&E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N-k}\widehat{\phi}_{0}](\tau)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-2n_{\beta}-2N+2k+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\psi}_{0}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi}_{0}]\Bigg]+C|\Phi_{0}|^{2}(1+\tau)^{-4-\beta_{0}-2N+2k+\delta}\end{split}

(10.12)

and moreover

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi_{0}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-2N+2k+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\psi}_{0}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi}_{0}]\Bigg]+C|\Phi_{0}|^{2}(1+\tau)^{-4-\beta_{0}-2N+2k+2\delta}.\end{split}

(10.13)

(ii)

if
\beta_{1}>2n_{\beta}

\begin{split}&E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N+n_{\beta}+1-k}({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(n_{\beta}+1)})_{\geq 1}](\tau)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-2n_{\beta}-2N+2k+\delta}r_{\infty}^{\max\{2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{1},0\}+\delta}\\
&\cdot\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{1,\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg],\end{split}

(10.14)

and moreover

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT^{N+n_{\beta}+1-k}({\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(n_{\beta}+1)})_{\geq 1})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-2n_{\beta}-2N+2k+\delta}r_{\infty}^{\max\{2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{1},0\}+\delta}\\
\cdot&\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{1,\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg],\end{split}

(10.15)

(iii)

if
\beta_{1}\leq 2n_{\beta}

\begin{split}&E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N+n_{\beta}-k}({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}](\tau)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2-2n_{\beta}-2N+2k}r_{\infty}^{2n_{\beta}-\beta_{1}+\delta}\\
&\cdot\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta})+1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2+\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+n_{\beta})\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg],\end{split}

(10.16)

and moreover

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(LT^{N+n_{\beta}-k}({\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2n_{\beta}-2N+2k}r_{\infty}^{2n_{\beta}-\beta_{1}+\delta}\\
&\cdot\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta})+1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2+\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+n_{\beta})\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg].\end{split}

(10.17)

Proof.

Consider first the spherical harmonic mode
\phi_{0}
. We can repeat the steps in the proof of Proposition
10.4
, using (
10.2
) to estimate the terms coming from the inhomogeneity in the equation for
\widehat{\phi}
. When considering
\phi_{0}
in the case
\beta_{0}>1
, we carry out estimates directly for
\widehat{\phi}_{0}
, in contrast with Proposition
10.4
.

Consider now the remaining spherical harmonic modes
\phi_{\geq 1}
. We will derive estimates for
({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1+n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}
. Since
(\square_{g}-V_{\alpha})({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1+n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}=0
, the energy boundedness estimate (
6.20
) and the integrated energy estimates in Corollary
6.8
apply.

Note that since
\beta_{1}>\beta_{0}
, we also have that

\beta_{1}>2n_{\beta}-1.

Suppose that
\beta_{1}>2n_{\beta}
. Then we proceed again as in the proof of Proposition
10.4
, but with
T^{1+n_{\beta}-k}({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(n_{\beta}+1)})_{\geq 1}
replacing
\phi_{0}
, but there is no inhomogeneity present in the relevant wave equation. The only difference is the following estimate along
\Sigma_{0}
:

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1+n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>Cr_{\infty}^{2n_{\beta}-\beta_{1}+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{1,\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+E_{T}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg].\end{split}

Now suppose that
\beta_{1}\leq 2n_{\beta}
. Then we apply the energy decay estimates in Proposition
8.3
to
({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}
instead of the energy decay estimates in Proposition
8.2
. In this case, we estimate the corresponding weighted energy along
\Sigma_{0}
as follows:

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta})+1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}}({\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(n_{\beta})})_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>Cr_{\infty}^{\max\{2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{1},0\}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta})+1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2+\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+E_{T}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg].\qed\end{split}

10.2
Additional
r
-weighted energy decay estimates

In Section
10.1
we established energy decay estimates for (inhomogeneous) solutions to (
3.1
) arising from cut-off initial data with growing weights in the quantity
r_{\infty}
, which appears in the support of the cut-off. In this section we promote these estimates to genuine energy decay estimates for
\widehat{\phi}
by interpolating between the decay estimates for the energies
E_{\mathbf{N}}
and the energies with additional growing
r
-weights in the integrands. We moreover derive decay estimates for appropriate
r
-weighted energies for
\widehat{\phi}
, both for positive and for negative
r
-weights.

We first establish energy decay estimates in the
\alpha<0
case:

Proposition 10.6
.

Let
\alpha<0
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\geq 0
and
p+\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}<2
and let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},N,\delta,p)>0
such that for all
0\leq k\leq 1

\begin{split}E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N-k}\widehat{\phi}]\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}-3-2N+2k+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]+|\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Bigg]\end{split}

(10.18)

and

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}&r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{p+\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}-3-2N+2k+\delta}\Biggl[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]+|\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Biggr].\end{split}

(10.19)

When restricting to
\phi=\phi_{\geq 1}
, the above estimates hold for
0\leq p<2
if we replace
\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}
and
\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}
with 1 and 0, respectively and we omit the term
|\Phi_{0}|^{2}
.

Proof.

First, recall that
T^{-1}\widehat{\phi}=\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}
in
v\leq v_{r_{\infty}}
.

We split

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}&r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R\leq r\leq\tau+R\}}r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq\tau+R\}}r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

First, choose
r_{\infty}=R+B\tau
, for some suitably large constant
B>0
. Then we can apply Proposition
10.4
to obtain

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R\leq r\leq\tau+R\}}r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{v\leq v_{\infty}\}\cap\{R\leq r\leq\tau+R\}}r^{p}(LT^{N+1-k}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{p}E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N+1-k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{p-3+\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}-2N+2k+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]+|\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Bigg].\end{split}

Note that we can moreover estimate analogously:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq\tau+R\}}&r^{2}(XT^{N-k}\widehat{\phi})^{2}+r^{2}\tilde{h}h(T^{N+1-k}\widehat{\phi})^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{N-k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
=&\>\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{v\leq v_{\infty}\}\cap\{r\leq\tau+R\}}r^{2}(XT^{N+1-k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}+r^{2}\tilde{h}h(T^{N+2-k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{N+1-k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N+1-k}\widehat{\phi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)}](\tau)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3+\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}-2N+2k+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]+|\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Bigg].\end{split}

We now partition the interval
[R+\tau,\infty)
into dyadic intervals
[r_{i},r_{i+1}]
, with
r_{0}=R+\tau
and, taking
B>0
suitably large, independently of
i
, we set
r_{\infty}=Br_{i+1}
. Then

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{i}\leq r\leq r_{i+1}\}}r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{v\leq v_{\infty}\}\cap\{r_{i}\leq r\leq r_{i+1}\}}r^{p}(LT^{N+1-k}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>Cr_{i}^{p-2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{v\leq v_{\infty}\}\cap\{r_{i}\leq r\leq r_{i+1}\}}r^{2}(LT^{N+1-k}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂},r_{\infty}}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-2N+2k+\delta}r_{i}^{\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}+p-2+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]+|\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Bigg].\end{split}

Using that
r_{i}\geq R+\tau
, for
p<2-\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}
we can find
\delta>0
suitably small, such that

\displaystyle r_{i}^{\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}+p-2+\delta}\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2+\max\{1-3\beta,0\}+p+2\delta}r_{i}^{-\delta}.

After summing over
i
and using the convergence of geometric series, we are then left with

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq\tau+R\}}r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{p-3+\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}-2N+2k+2\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]+|\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Bigg].\qed\end{split}

We now establish energy decay estimates in the
\alpha>0
case:

Proposition 10.7
.

Let
\alpha>0
,
\beta_{0}\neq 2l+1
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
0\leq p<2-\max\{2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{1},0\}
and let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},N,\delta)>0
such that for all
0\leq k\leq n_{\beta}+1
:

(i)

for all
0\leq p\leq 2
,

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}&r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\widehat{\psi}_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{p-3-2n_{\beta}-2N+2k+\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\psi}_{0}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi}_{0}]+\Phi_{0}^{2}\Bigg].\end{split}

(10.20)

(ii)

if
\beta_{1}>2n_{\beta}

\begin{split}&E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N-k}\phi_{\geq 1}](\tau)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-2n_{\beta}+2k+\max\{2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{1},0\}+\delta-2N}\\
&\cdot\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+1+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{1,\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg],\end{split}

(10.21)

and moreover, for
0\leq p<2-\max\{2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{1},0\}
,

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\psi_{\geq 1})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{p-3-2n_{\beta}+2k+\max\{2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{1},0\}+\delta-2N}\\
\cdot&\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta}+1)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{1,\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+1)\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}+1\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg],\end{split}

(10.22)

(iii)

if
\beta_{1}\leq 2n_{\beta}

\begin{split}&E_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{N-k}\phi_{\geq 1}](\tau)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2-\beta_{1}+2k+\delta-2N}\\
&\cdot\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta})+1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2+\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+n_{\beta})\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg],\end{split}

(10.23)

and moreover, for
0\leq p<2-2n_{\beta}+\beta_{1}
,

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p}(LT^{N-k}\psi_{\geq 1})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{p-2-\beta_{1}+2k+\delta-2N}\\
&\cdot\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+n_{\beta})+1\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2+\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+n_{\beta})\\
n_{1}\leq N+n_{\beta}\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-n_{\beta}}{\phi}_{\geq 1}]\Bigg].\end{split}

(10.24)

Proof.

We partition the interval
[R,\infty)
and apply the same interpolation argument as in the proof of Proposition
10.6
, appealing to the decay estimates in Proposition
10.5
. We omit the details.
∎

Now we can established improved decay rates for
r
-weighted energies with
decaying
weights in
r
.

We first introduce the following weighted initial energies along
\Sigma_{0}
involving time integrals of
\phi
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\beta_{0},N,\delta}^{(1)}[\phi]:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+2)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+2\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{\min\{3\beta_{0},1\}-\delta}|L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}\widehat{\psi}_{0}|^{2}+r^{1-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+2)\\
n_{1}\leq N+2\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{\phi}]+\mathfrak{I}_{\beta}^{2}[\phi]\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{when $\beta_{0}<1$},

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\beta_{0},N,\delta}^{(n_{\beta}+1)}[\phi]:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+2n_{\beta}+2)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+2n_{\beta}+2\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\psi}_{0}|^{2}

\displaystyle+r^{\min\{\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta},1\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}+r^{2+\min\{\beta_{1}-2n_{\beta},0\}-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-n_{\beta}}{\psi}_{\geq 1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+2n_{\beta}+2)\\
n_{1}\leq N+2n_{\beta}+2\end{subarray}}E_{\mathbf{N}}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi}]+\mathfrak{I}_{\beta}^{2}[\phi]\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{when $\beta_{0}>1$}.

The above energies will play a role in the energy decay estimates in Proposition
10.8
.

Proposition 10.8
.

Let
\beta_{0}\notin 2\mathbb{N}_{0}+1
and
\delta>0
arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,h,V_{\alpha},N,\delta)>0
such that for all
0\leq k\leq n_{\beta}+1
:

(i)

when
\alpha<0

(\beta_{0}<1)
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1+2\beta_{0}}(XT^{N-k}w_{0}^{-1}\widehat{\phi}_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(1+\tau)^{\max\{1-3\beta_{0},0\}+\beta_{0}-5+2k-2N+\delta}\mathbf{D}_{\beta_{0},N,\delta}^{(1)}[\phi],

(10.25)

and

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}(T^{N-k}\widehat{\phi}_{\geq 1})^{2}+r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT^{N-k}\widehat{\phi}_{\geq 1})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-\beta_{0}+2k-2N+2\delta}\mathbf{D}_{\beta_{0},N,\delta}^{(1)}[\phi],\end{split}

(ii)

and when
\alpha>0

(\beta_{0}>1)
:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}(T^{N-k}\widehat{\phi})^{2}+r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT^{N-k}\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-\beta_{0}-2n_{\beta}+2k+\max\{2n_{\beta}+1-\beta_{1},0\}-2N+2\delta}\mathbf{D}_{\beta_{0},N,\delta}^{(n_{\beta}+1)}[\phi].\end{split}

(10.26)

Proof.

We prove the
k=0
case here. The
1\leq k\leq 1+n_{\beta}
case proceeds entirely analogously. Note that
\widehat{\phi}
satisfies the equation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\widehat{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}=

\displaystyle\>\widehat{F}[T{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}],

\displaystyle F[T\widehat{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}]:=

\displaystyle\>2r(1-h)XT\widehat{\psi}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}+\frac{dh}{dr}r^{2}T\widehat{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}+r^{2}h\tilde{h}T^{2}\widehat{\phi}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}+r^{2}\square_{g}\Phi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}.

Consider first
\beta_{0}>1
. By Corollary
9.7
with
q=-\beta_{0}+\delta
, we have that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}\widehat{\phi}^{2}+r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(X\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}(\mathcal{L}\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT\widehat{\psi})^{2}+r^{-2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(T\widehat{\phi})^{2}+r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}(r^{2}\tilde{h}h)^{2}(T^{2}\widehat{\phi})^{2}\\
&+r^{4-\beta_{0}+\delta}(\square_{g}\Phi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr.\end{split}

Note that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{4-\beta_{0}+\delta}(\square_{g}\Phi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau)^{-4-2\beta_{0}+2\delta}.

We apply Corollary
9.7
once more, with
q=-\beta_{0}+\delta+2
to estimate further

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}&r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}(L\hat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT\widehat{\psi})^{2}+r^{-2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(T\widehat{\phi})^{2}+r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}(r^{2}\tilde{h}h)^{2}(T^{2}\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{4-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT^{2}\widehat{\psi})^{2}+r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}(T^{2}\widehat{\phi})^{2}+r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(r^{2}\tilde{h}h)^{2}(T^{3}\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{6-\beta_{0}+\delta}(\square_{g}T\Phi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr,\end{split}

and we have that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{6-\beta_{0}+\delta}(\square_{g}T\Phi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau)^{-4-2\beta_{0}+2\delta}.

By definition of
n_{\beta}
, we have that
\beta_{0}-1<2n_{\beta}<\beta_{0}+1
. If
2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}>0
, we apply Corollary
9.7
a total of
n_{\beta}
times to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}&r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}\widehat{\phi}^{2}+r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(X\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT^{n_{\beta}}\widehat{\psi})^{2}+r^{2n_{\beta}-4-\beta_{0}+\delta}(T^{n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi})^{2}+r^{2n_{\beta}-2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(r^{2}\tilde{h}h)^{2}(T^{1+n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau)^{-4-2\beta+2\delta}\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT^{n_{\beta}}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi}](\tau)+C\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau)^{-4-2\beta_{0}+2\delta}.\end{split}

If
2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}<0
, we apply Corollary
9.7
a total of
n_{\beta}+1
times to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}&r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}\widehat{\phi}^{2}+r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(X\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2n_{\beta}+2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT^{n_{\beta}+1}\widehat{\psi})^{2}+r^{2n_{\beta}-2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(T^{n_{\beta}+1}\widehat{\phi})^{2}+r^{2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}+\delta}(r^{2}\tilde{h}h)^{2}(T^{2+n_{\beta}}\widehat{\phi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+C\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau)^{-4-2\beta+2\delta}\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}+2+\delta}(XT^{n_{\beta}+1}\widehat{\psi})^{2}\,d\sigma dr+CE_{\mathbf{N}}[T^{n_{\beta}+1}\widehat{\phi}](\tau)+C\Phi_{0}^{2}(1+\tau)^{-4-2\beta_{0}+2\delta}.\end{split}

We can therefore apply the
r
-weighted energy decay estimates in Proposition
10.7
and
(i)
of Proposition
10.5
, with
p=2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}+\delta
and
N=n_{\beta}
(if
2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}>0
) or
p=2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}+2+\delta
and
N=n_{\beta}+1
(if
2n_{\beta}-\beta_{0}<0
).

We then obtain the following estimate for
\phi_{0}
with
\beta_{0}>1
:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}&r^{-\beta_{0}+\delta}(T^{N}\widehat{\phi}_{0})^{2}+r^{2-\beta_{0}+\delta}(XT^{N}\widehat{\phi}_{0})^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-\beta_{0}-2n_{\beta}+2\delta-2N}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq 2(N+2n_{\beta}+2)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N+2n_{\beta}+2\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}L(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{n_{3}-1-n_{\beta}}\widehat{\psi}_{0}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1}+n_{2}\leq 3(N+2n_{\beta}+2)\\
